Company - Management

C6311 questions
C-5568C-5663C-5770C-57b55C-5855C-5970C-6062C-6170C-6262C-6357C-6460

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

AQ is run by a disciplined, candid operator-CEO inside a highly congruent, decentralised, cost-focused culture, with an outstanding record of profit delivery and acquisition discipline across cycles, and with the weaknesses of its virtues: lean organisations stretch during surges, revenue ambitions run ahead of delivery, and governance of pay remains founder-controlled. Management quality is the main reason the operational (rather than structural) competitive advantage has endured, so in this case it carries unusual weight in the forecast.

For the Consumer, the management evidence supports four modelling choices: trust the margin floor (above 8 percent through downturns, about 10 percent in normal conditions) because this team has defended it for 14 quarters and three decades of profitability; distrust the revenue targets (15 percent) and model growth from end-market and capacity evidence; expect operational problems during the ramp to be fixed within two to four quarters (precedent) but with real friction on delivery, cash and acquired-site margins in the meantime; and assume no capital-return or leverage change in the base case, with acquisitions continuing at a disciplined 2 to 4 percent of revenue a year. The site-level leadership bench, not capital or demand, is the scarce resource that will set how fast new capacity converts into profit.

C-55

How big is the company's ambition or vision, particularly in relation to peers?

C68

Question asked How big is the company's ambition or vision (a force: growth as a priority, outcompeting, innovating, adapting, pushing), particularly in relation to peers?

Judgement: big and long-standing, framed in financial outputs rather than grand narrative: grow above 15 percent a year, double EPS every five years, earn above 10 percent EBT, deliver 98 percent on time with 100 percent quality. The ambition is higher than most Nordic contract-manufacturing peers' and matches the most ambitious (HANZA's 15 percent-plus growth; Scanfil's new 15 percent growth target with a lower 7 to 8 percent margin; Note's 9.5 to 10.5 percent margin target).

  • Growth as a priority: the 15 percent target (10 organic, 5 acquired) has been kept through two misses; the CEO publicly pledged in April 2024 to "eat his hat" if AQ failed to reach SEK 10bn of sales (missed at SEK 8.55bn); the next EPS doubling is targeted by 2031.
  • Outcompeting and adapting: entry into medium-voltage data-centre transformers and solid-state transformer prototypes; taking design responsibility; capacity doubled in a quarter; buying Norlake to double US capacity faster than building.
  • Calibration: the ambition is credible on margin and EPS (both met) and over-reaching on revenue (both 2024 and 2025 missed); it is ambition backed by discipline (no overpaying for growth) rather than ambition at any price, which is the right kind for a compounder.
C-56

How great and smart is the strategy, with right and bold strategic priorities?

C63

Question asked How great and smart is the strategy, with right and bold strategic priorities (an enabler): widening the moat over the mid term, growth as a priority, and a dynamic strategy that can capitalise on a plausible range of environments?

Judgement: smart, coherent and dynamic, but not bold. The strategy widens the moat slowly (design responsibility, technology content), keeps growth as a priority, and is built to work across environments (organic growth and capacity investment in upturns; cash release and cheap acquisitions in downturns). Its weaknesses are what it does not attempt: pricing, balance-sheet use and early lock-in of next-generation data-centre designs.

  • Right priorities: concentrate investment where demand exceeds capacity (data centres, defence, grid); local-for-local footprint as nearshoring accelerates; acquire distressed capacity at about 6 times EBITA and fill it with group volume; standardise systems (Monitor ERP) across acquisitions.
  • Dynamic across environments: the counter-cyclical working-capital and acquisition pattern means a downturn strengthens AQ relative to leveraged rivals; the 2025 pause and 2026 restart show the discipline in action.
  • Not bold: no step change in acquisitions despite SEK 0.8bn-plus of net cash; no buybacks; reactive rather than anticipatory capacity (Norlake bought once Virginia was full; test equipment after quality failures); dependence on one gatekeeper customer in data centres; 800V DC positioning still at prototype stage while the sector digest names 2026-2028 as the re-award window.
  • Wisdom Library check: the strategy fits "sector tailwind plus a self-improving company" and "core versus adjacency growth" (close adjacencies), not "massive corporate change".
C-57

How great is the chief executive, judged by actions rather than conjecture?

C70

Question asked How great is the chief executive, judged by actions rather than conjecture (an enabler, not an ossifier)?

Judgement: a very good operator-CEO and an enabler, judged by five years of actions, with blemishes on revenue promises and on keeping execution standards through the current ramp.

  • Background: KTH mechanical engineer; ten years running industrial companies in China for Xylem, Huber+Suhner and AQ; AQ Injection Moulding business-area manager 2011-2019; CEO since April 2021, chosen for cultural fit with the decentralised model.
  • Actions and outcomes: revenue SEK 5.47bn to SEK 9.07bn (2021-2025, plus 65.8 percent); EPS SEK 4.00 to SEK 7.38; EBIT margin 7.1 to 9.3 percent (10.1 percent in Q3 2026); EPS doubled within five years as targeted; net cash maintained; refused acquisitions in 2025 when prices were too high, then bought Time 24, Gumboda and Norlake cheaply; doubled data-centre transformer output in a quarter; replaced the India leadership and dispatched Rockford staff to Time 24 within the quarter problems surfaced; called 90.5 and 91.35 percent delivery precision "really disappointing" in 2023 and fixed it then.
  • Blemishes: the missed SEK 10bn pledge; FY2025 organic growth of 2 percent against HANZA's double digits; Q4 2025 quality costs that the Leadership report says management downplayed; delivery precision now at 91 percent under his watch.
  • External assessment: DNB Carnegie management-quality score 9 out of 10 and target credibility 5 out of 5 (May 2026). Archetype: the disciplined operator CEO (not the "animal" CEO), suited to a compounder.
C-57b

How high a priority is hiring well for the chief executive, and how great is the leadership team the chief executive has built?

C55

Question asked How high a priority is hiring well for the chief executive, and how great is the leadership team the chief executive has built (an enabler)?

Judgement: hiring and promoting from within is a visible priority and the core team is solid, but the bench of site-level leaders is the binding constraint on growth today.

  • Strengths: CFO Christina Hegg (chartered accountant for 20-plus years, strict cash control, 25,000 shares); a nine-person group management (six men, three women) with internal succession (Paulina Duszel for Electric Cabinets and System Products in 2025); five young local leaders promoted in Q1 2026 in Sweden, Italy and Bulgaria; strong local managers where they exist (Dan Day's Virginia business is "highly successful"; mdexx turnaround).
  • Weaknesses: the Q2 2026 Earnings Review's sceptical reading that the deficits in India, Mexico and New York show "a severe shortage of capable local leaders"; reliance on the same few managers for multiple jobs (Dan Day running Virginia and Norlake; Rockford staff sent to the UK); no formal succession plan for the CEO and CFO (Leadership report key-person section); 20 percent employee turnover.
  • Model implication: the pace at which new capacity (Norlake, 2027 additions) converts into revenue at group margins depends on leaders the company has not yet shown it has in sufficient number; this argues for gradual, not immediate, margin convergence on new sites.
C-58

How well resourced and invested is the whole business?

C55

Question asked How well resourced and invested is the whole business (a buffer; an ant colony: built yesterday for resilience)?

Judgement: financially very well buffered, operationally thinly buffered. The balance sheet is an "ant colony" buffer built for resilience; the operating organisation is lean by culture and currently stretched by the ramp.

  • Financial buffers: net cash SEK 0.8bn-plus excluding leases; equity ratio 67 to 68 percent; 117 consecutive profitable quarters; self-funded capacity investment.
  • Investment: capex SEK 241m in FY2025 (about 2.6 percent of sales including intangibles) and SEK 201m in nine months of 2026; Hungary SEK 23m; northern Sweden SEK 60m; Wytheville USD 3.7m; test equipment and vacuum impregnation; robotic welding and powder coating in Bulgaria; solar at Indian and Bulgarian sites.
  • Thin operational buffers: all data-centre factories running "full throttle"; delivery precision 91 percent; inventory turns 2.9; India quality deficits; 20 percent staff turnover; a lean head office; capacity added reactively when sites fill rather than ahead of demand.
  • Net: sufficient to sustain growth but with friction; the recurring AQ pattern is bottlenecks during surges resolved by local reinvestment within a few quarters.
C-59

How holistic and congruent is the culture, with alignment across the total system?

C70

Question asked How holistic and congruent is the culture, with alignment across the total system (as at Lifco, Avanza or Ryanair)? Such that the goals, strategy, culture, execution, decisions are congruent, holistic and symbiotic?

Judgement: highly congruent, in the Lifco mould of a decentralised acquirer with a cost culture, with one incongruent element in long-term pay.

  • Congruence: stated values (simplicity, cost-effectiveness, respect, "we dare to go our own way and say it like it is") match the structure (more than 40 profit-accountable operating companies, a small head office), the strategy (local-for-local, buy cheap, fill with volume), the incentives (short-term bonus 80 percent on EBT, 20 percent on inventory turns), the capital structure (no debt, equity ratio far above target), the communication (admitting problems publicly) and the record (117 profitable quarters).
  • Strains: internal labour-principles self-assessment 3.74 out of 4 (2024) down from 3.83 (2021); 20 percent staff turnover; decentralisation can let local quality degrade undetected (India; late-2025 transformer defects).
  • Incongruent element: warrant programmes with no performance conditions, no clawback and retention after leaving, set by a founder-only remuneration committee; this rewards share-price moves rather than the EBT, cash and inventory discipline the rest of the system enforces.
C-60

How clear, straightforward and helpful is the company's communication, including on the strategy and the driver transmission mechanism?

C62

Judgement: clear, candid and helpful on strategy, targets and operational problems; deliberately unhelpful on near-term numbers and segment profitability, which hides part of the transmission mechanism.

  • Helpful: targets stated in P&L terms (EBT margin, growth, equity ratio, EPS doubling); operational KPIs published quarterly (delivery precision, inventory turns, quality); problems named and explained (India, Mexico and Plattsburgh, Time 24, the Q2 cash drain, defence delays and their October 2026 fix); capacity metrics given (70 transformer systems a month; Norlake's USD 35m potential; Virginia's USD 50m ceiling); currency effects and sensitivities disclosed.
  • Unhelpful: no numeric near-term guidance (by policy); no segment or product-area margins, so the claim that data-centre and design-responsible products carry higher margins cannot be verified; Q3 2026 materials gave two different prior-year EPS figures; the data-centre customer concentration (about 50 percent of segment sales) emerged only in a call answer.
  • Net: a modeller can see what moves revenue and the group margin, but must infer the mix effect.
C-61

How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking?

C70

Question asked How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?

Judgement: very strong over three decades and over the current CEO's tenure, especially in tough environments; the gaps are in revenue ambitions and operational KPIs, not in profit delivery.

  • Delivery: positive net profit every quarter since 1994 (117 quarters), including Q2 2020 (organic minus 21 percent) and the 2024 truck downturn; EBT above 8 percent for 14 consecutive quarters; EPS doubled 2021 to 2026; margin target raised and met within one quarter; data-centre output doubled sequentially; acquisition discipline held through 2025 and acted on in 2026; mdexx turned from minus 7 to plus 7 percent EBT in a year.
  • Outperforming in tough conditions: EPS rose in FY2024 (SEK 6.91 to 7.27) while organic revenue fell 6.9 percent, and margin rose to 9.6 percent; Note, Incap and Componenta struggled in the same period.
  • Misses: total growth below the 15 percent target in 2024 and 2025; the SEK 10bn pledge; delivery precision, inventory turns and quality below target; defence sales growth of 18 percent in 2025 that disappointed management.
C-62

To what extent does the company hold outlier standards, driving higher, faster and better, with a record of out-executing competitors?

C62

Judgement: outlier standards in what it targets and in cost and speed of execution; not outlier in what it currently achieves on delivery and quality. AQ demonstrably out-executes Nordic contract-manufacturing peers on profitability and resilience.

  • Out-execution: EBIT margin 9.3 to 10 percent against a 7 to 8 percent norm (Note 7.7 percent, Incap 8.8 percent in Q2 2026; HANZA under 4 percent in 2016-2022; Componenta under 5 percent); best-in-class returns against Scanfil and Inission; Time 24 integrated "at rocket speed"; transformer output doubled in a quarter.
  • Standards: 98 percent delivery, 100 percent quality and 3.5 inventory turns are demanding targets kept unchanged in July 2026 despite misses, which is the right signal; achievement lags (91.1 percent, 99.6 percent, 2.9).
C-63

Explain, with the evidence, the extent to which the company puts shareholders first, ahead of management, and how prudent its governance is: what does the evidence show, and why?

C57

Question asked Explain, with the evidence, the extent to which the company puts shareholders first, ahead of management, and how prudent its governance is (scaffolding put in place even where it was not needed): what does the evidence show, and why?

Judgement: AQ puts shareholders first in substance (owner-operators with large personal stakes, conservative accounting, no dilution, disciplined capital) and its governance is prudent in finance and improving in board structure, but it lags best practice on executive pay and committee independence. The reason is its history: a founder-owned company run by and for owners, now institutionalising after Large Cap entry, with the founders still controlling the pay and nomination processes.

  • Shareholder-first evidence: founders own 34.3 percent of shares (Mellgren alone 15.7m shares); CEO holds 114,840 shares and CFO 25,000; no insider selling in the last twelve months; single share class; no adjusted earnings measures; zero capitalised R&D no equity raises; acquisitions bought cheaply; acquisitions refused when overpriced; a progressive dividend; CEO total pay SEK 6.52m (modest for a Large Cap); unqualified EY audits; no related-party loans or litigation.
  • Prudent scaffolding beyond need: equity ratio held near 67 percent against a 40 percent floor; no derivatives; conservative amortisation lives.
  • Against: warrants with no performance criteria, clawbacks or post-vesting holding, retained after employment ends (Incap and Kitron impose holding periods and clawbacks); a remuneration committee of the two non-independent founders (a Code violation); a nomination committee including both founders (a Code deviation); a director serving since 1998; no buybacks ever and cash accumulating; zero currency hedging, which is prudent on cost but leaves reported earnings exposed.
  • Net: alignment is strong through ownership; process governance is mid-modernisation (independent chairman, 83 percent board independence, fully independent audit committee) and the remaining gaps sit exactly where founders retain control.
C-64

To what extent is the company an enlightened capital allocator and a dynamic repurchaser of its shares, not just an operator or generator?

C60

Question asked To what extent is the company an enlightened capital allocator and a dynamic repurchaser of its shares, not just an operator or generator, with a record of allocating capital at a high return on new invested capital?

Judgement: an excellent allocator on the acquisition side (price discipline, counter-cyclical timing, turnarounds that lift returns), an ordinary one on surplus cash (accumulated, not returned), and not a repurchaser at all.

  • Allocation record: eight acquisitions since 2019 at about 6 times EBITA; after-tax ROIC from 11.9 percent (2019) to 16.7 percent (2025) while acquiring, the opposite of the Halma drift; capacity investment concentrated in the highest-return niches; refused deals in 2025; Norlake bought for little more than its liabilities and property to double US capacity.
  • Surplus cash: net cash grew through 2025 to about SEK 700m by Q1 2026 and SEK 817m excluding leases by Q2; the dividend pays about 20 to 25 percent of earnings; the company has never bought back shares, citing low liquidity, an argument weaker since Large Cap entry with USD 4 to 6m of daily value traded.
  • Not dynamic: management does not treat its own shares as an investment alternative even at times of low valuation (the share was near SEK 132 in early 2025); returns are diluted by idle cash.

Risks

❌ D474 questions
C-8075C-8145C-8235C-8338

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

AQ's fundamental risk profile is moderate and well buffered (net cash, 117 profitable quarters, a margin that held through 2020 and 2024), while its share-price risk is high, because the premium multiple rests on a data-centre narrative that is currently the market's main worry. The growth engine has accelerated, not decelerated, and no fundamental risk threatens solvency; the material fundamental risks cluster around the data-centre ramp (one customer at about 50 percent of segment sales, quality in novel high-voltage products, hyperscaler capex financing) and around a synchronised European industrial downturn compounding with input-cost lags.

For the Consumer the risk work translates into explicit model scenarios: a base case close to consensus; a cyclical downside in which the base falls about 10 percent and EBIT 10 to 20 percent for a year (with an 8 percent EBIT margin as the floor); a data-centre downside in which that line stalls or loses its main customer (group EBIT minus 5 to 10 percent and a narrative break); and a perception case in which nothing changes in the P&L but the multiple returns to its five-year low (about minus 25 percent). The predictability judgement in the third set should weigh that the earnings cone is moderate while the valuation cone is wide.

C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

B75

Judgement: the opposite has happened: the growth engine has accelerated sharply over the last twelve months and the input change force (data-centre and defence demand) is still strengthening. The deceleration risk is forward-looking (rate of change peaking from Q1 2027), not present.

  • Twelve months: organic growth from 2.0 percent (FY2025; Q4 2025 roughly in line) to 6.3, 9.1 and 17.9 percent in Q1-Q3 2026; total revenue growth 21.4 percent in Q3 2026. FY2025 (6 percent total, 2 percent organic) was well below AQ's normal rate (about 10 percent compound revenue growth FY2019-25; a 10 percent organic target); 2026 is running above it.
  • Input force: data-centre inductive growth more than 60 percent in Q3 2026 and faster than management expected; defence demand strong with deliveries delayed rather than lost; grid recovering. The cyclical component (restocking, US short-cycle) is the part likely to slow first.
  • Forward watch: the memo's alarm levels (organic growth below 10 percent for two quarters; data-centre share falling sequentially) are the right early warnings.
C-81

What fundamental risks are there that are sudden, high-magnitude and low-probability, and how plausible and how large is each?

❌ D45

Judgement: the sudden fundamental risks are concentrated in the data-centre line (customer, quality, capex) and in a synchronised European industrial recession; individually they are low-to-medium probability over 18 months, and none threatens solvency, but two of them would also break the narrative that supports the multiple.

Ranked by expected impact (plausibility over 18 months; magnitude; my estimates where calculated):

  1. Synchronised European heavy-vehicle and industrial recession (medium plausibility): Volvo and Scania cut production together while copper and steel prices lag; base revenue down about 10 percent; EBIT down 10 to 20 percent for a year (the Risks report's severe case: 2027 sales SEK 9,000m, 8 percent EBIT margin, EBIT SEK 720m, about 37 percent below consensus). History (2020, 2024) suggests AQ's margins hold better than this case assumes.
  2. Loss, redesign or dual-sourcing at the customer that is about 50 percent of data-centre sales (low-to-medium): data-centre sales are about 6 to 8 percent of group revenue but a larger share of profit growth; a loss would cut group EBIT by perhaps 5 to 10 percent (estimate) and, more importantly, the growth narrative.
  3. Quality catastrophe in medium-voltage transformers (medium; it has happened once, late 2025): warranty and redesign provisions, customer compensation, reputational damage in a market where the scarce asset is a qualified design slot; the Risks report rates it the highest-plausibility failure mode.
  4. Hyperscaler capex halt or financing crunch (low-to-medium, rising): transmission to AQ's inductive division within two quarters; data-centre revenue down 30 to 50 percent at worst, scaled-up factories underutilised.
  5. Input-cost spike with lag (medium, moderate magnitude): 50 to 100 basis points of margin for one to two quarters, recovered later (FY2022 precedent).
  6. 800V DC and solid-state transformer bypass (low within 18 months, medium for 2028-30): the data-centre line plateaus or declines from 2028.
  7. Lower-order risks: tariff escalation affecting Mexico and Canada; a plant disaster (the Riedel hailstorm precedent); dual departure of CEO and CFO without a succession plan; a failed acquisition integration (Time 24 is small). - Correlation: risks 2, 3 and 4 share one root (the data-centre ramp) and risk 1 compounds with input-cost lags, so the joint downside is fatter than the individual probabilities suggest.
C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

❌ D35

Judgement: perception risk is high and partly crystallising now; it is the most likely source of a large share-price fall in the next six months, independent of AQ's results.

  • Already visible: the price fell from SEK 248.0 (6 October 2026) to SEK 226.6 (23 October), about minus 9 percent, while FY2027 consensus EPS rose 8 percent in a month and Q3 beat EBIT forecasts by 16.7 percent; the 24-month-forward P/E fell from the top decile of its five-year range to the median. That is a de-rating driven by the market, not the company.
  • The live trigger, the AI-financing scare: neocloud and hyperscaler credit spreads widening, technology net selling the heaviest since January 2025, Goldman Sachs' risk-appetite indicator negative; AQ's premium rests on its data-centre narrative, so any hyperscaler capex guidance cut, a large electrical vendor's warning, or an 800V DC announcement could take 10 to 15 percent off in a week (memo).
  • Regime tilts: the weekly digest has Momentum at Underweight and Fragility at Double Underweight; the Wisdom Library's "small-cap growth cracks before large caps" and "in a bear market the cleanest case in the sector wins" both argue that a momentum-A, premium-multiple, complex (17 countries, 73 percent unhedged currency) mid-cap is sold before simpler defensives.
  • Sector read-across: the Industry digest's thesis that unprotected data-centre revenue deserves a lower multiple (Halma's photonics, Volex's short contracts and cash conversion) could be applied to AQ by the market at any time; peer warnings (Incap, Note) or a Volex or Huber+Suhner disappointment could spill over.
  • Stock-specific: a first founder block sale after a large rise; thin, uniform coverage (four visible brokers, all Hold, no buy-side champion among them); a governance headline on warrants.
C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

❌ D38

Judgement: about 25 percent with earnings intact (to roughly SEK 165 to 175) and about 35 to 40 percent if a cyclical earnings cut coincides with the de-rating (to roughly SEK 140); a panic case near SEK 125 is possible but would not be a fair trough for this business. All figures are calculated from the block's valuation series and current consensus.

  • Starting point: SEK 226.6; 24-month-forward P/E 20.83 times (49th percentile of five years), implying 24-month-forward EPS of about SEK 10.88 (calculated); dashboard P/E 22.4 times on SEK 10.13 at the 77th percentile of ten years.
  • Trough with earnings intact: five-year low 15.54 times and the ten-year "crash" band 15.7 times, times SEK 10.88, is about SEK 169 to 171, minus 25 percent. Cross-check: Nordic contract manufacturers at 12 to 13 times EV/EBIT applied to FY2027 consensus EBIT of SEK 1,150m plus net cash of about SEK 0.7bn gives SEK 158 to 171. The memo's trough (15.5 times on about SEK 11.3 of FY2028-weighted earnings) was about SEK 175.
  • Trough with a cyclical cut: EPS 15 percent below consensus (about SEK 9.25) at 15.5 times is about SEK 143, minus 37 percent, matching the Risks report's SEK 140 (15 times EV/EBIT on SEK 720m of 2027 EBIT) and the early-2025 price of SEK 132.
  • Panic case: 13.5 times (the old ten-year 10th percentile band) on the cut earnings is about SEK 125, minus 45 percent.
  • Fair trough: for a 10 percent-margin, 10 percent-growth, net-cash compounder, 15 to 16 times forward earnings is a fair trough; the downside is mainly a multiple question while earnings revisions are positive.

Guidance

C566 questions
C-6855C-7055C-7155C-7245C-8564C-7355

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

AQ guides only through long-term output targets and refuses near-term numbers, which produces a consistent pattern: the margin target is credible and now conservative (EBT above 10 percent, already reached), the growth target is aspirational (15 percent, missed in 2024 and 2025), and good news reaches the market as beats rather than upgrades. Consensus has calibrated itself sensibly to this: it adopts the margin target, discounts the growth target, and broadly matches the EPS-doubling ambition for the next three years.

For the Consumer the guidance work implies: do not use 15 percent growth as a forecast; use the margin target as a normal-conditions floor; read management's informal commentary (capacity doubling, data-centre demand ahead of expectations, Q4 backlog recovery, 2027 capacity additions) as evidence that consensus revenue for FY2027-28 may be conservative on the structural slice, offset by caution on the base and on the sustainability of Q3's utilisation-assisted margin; and remember that consensus excludes acquisitions the company intends to make, which is a structural source of upward revision if the cadence resumes.

C-68

To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

C55

Judgement: there is no numeric guidance for FY2026 or FY2027; against the long-term targets, consensus sits in line with the margin target and below the growth target, which is the right calibration given the company's record.

  • Margin: target EBT above 10 percent; consensus EBT margin 9.8 percent for FY2026 (pre-tax SEK 1,001m on revenue of SEK 10,226m, calculated), 10.1 percent for FY2027 and 10.3 percent for FY2028. Nine-month 2026 EBT margin 9.81 percent; Q3 10.0 percent. Brokers have adopted the target because it has been reached.
  • Growth: target above 15 percent a year; consensus revenue growth 12.7 percent (FY2026), 11.2 percent (FY2027), 7.5 percent (FY2028). Brokers discount the target because it was missed in 2024 (minus 4.6 percent) and 2025 (plus 6.0 percent) and because consensus excludes unannounced acquisitions (the target assumes 5 percent acquired growth a year).
  • EPS ambition: doubling every five years is about 14.9 percent a year; consensus FY2025-28 EPS growth is 14.0 percent a year (calculated), i.e. consensus is broadly aligned with the ambition for three years and below it beyond (FY2029, one broker, plus 6.8 percent).
  • Why this pattern: the company sets aspirational multi-year targets and refuses near-term guidance; brokers anchor on demonstrated delivery (margin) and discount aspiration (growth).
C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

C55

Judgement: AQ rarely upgrades formally and does so only after long evidence; it prefers to beat silently. The CEO's informal ambitions on revenue have over-reached.

  • Formal upgrades: the EBT target was raised once (July 2026) after 13 consecutive quarters above the old 8 percent target, described by the CEO as "a natural step"; the growth, equity-ratio and EPS-doubling targets have been reaffirmed, not raised.
  • Behaviour: no short-term guidance to upgrade; positive surprises reach the market as beats (Q2 2026 EBIT 6 percent above consensus; Q3 2026 16.7 percent above DNB Carnegie); the sell-side calls the refusal to guide the mechanism behind regular beats (the Wisdom Library's "sandbagging precision management" archetype fits in part).
  • CEO's informal record: the April 2024 SEK 10bn sales pledge was missed by about 15 percent; the five-year EPS doubling was achieved.
  • Implication: upgrades will come through consensus revisions after results, not from the company; a further target raise is plausible only after a full year above 10 percent EBT (no full year has yet exceeded 9.6 percent).
C-71

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

C55

Judgement: the margin target is conservative relative to recent delivery and the improved mix; the growth target is ambitious relative to the last five years but closer to achievable now that the input change forces (data-centre power, defence) are stronger than in any recent period; the EPS ambition is realistic.

  • Margin: EBT averaged 8.2 percent in 2021-2024, 9.2 percent in 2025 and 9.8 percent in nine months of 2026; a target of "above 10 percent" is a floor that the company already touches, with mix (inductive from 6 to 25 percent of revenue 2018-2025; data-centre share rising) moving in its favour. Conservative after adjusting for the input forces.
  • Growth: revenue grew 10.0 percent a year FY2019-25 including acquisitions and a price-led boom; organic growth was 20.7 percent (2023), minus 6.9 percent (2024) and 2.0 percent (2025), an average of about 5 percent. The 10 percent organic target has not been delivered through a cycle; with data-centre growth above 60 percent and defence and grid support, 10 percent organic is plausible for 2026-27 but not as a through-cycle rate. Acquired growth of 5 percent a year has been delivered only in 2025 (7.2 percent, mainly mdexx) and not in 2026 to date (1 to 2 percent).
  • EPS doubling in five years: delivered once at a time of a large margin rise; repeating it needs about 14 to 15 percent a year with little margin help and a higher tax rate, so it now depends on growth and acquisitions.
C-72

How specific is the guidance: built from stated building blocks, or vague about how results come about?

❌ D45

Judgement: the targets are specific in output terms but are not built from stated building blocks; the company gives useful operating metrics but no segment economics, so the modeller must construct the bridge.

  • Specific: EBT margin above 10 percent; growth above 15 percent split 10 organic and 5 acquired; equity ratio above 40 percent; dividend about 25 percent of profit; EPS doubling every five years; KPI targets (98 percent delivery, 3.5 inventory turns, 100 percent quality).
  • Partial building blocks: capacity data (70 transformer systems a month in Q2 2026; Virginia's USD 50m ceiling; Norlake up to USD 35m; battery-enclosure factories up to EUR 40m); data-centre share of sales (6 percent in Q2 2026); defence share (5 to 7 percent, a broker estimate); currency sensitivities; acquisition cadence (two to four a year).
  • Missing: segment or product-area margins; data-centre growth or margin guidance; how the 10 percent EBT is built (mix versus utilisation versus cost); acquired-revenue margins and convergence timetable; tax-rate guidance beyond the Pillar II timing.
C-85

To what extent is informal/qualitative company commentary above or below formal guidance and sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

C64

Judgement: informal commentary is above consensus on the growth slice and on the near term (Q4 2026), and in line-to-cautious on the cyclical base and on the sustainability of the Q3 margin. On balance it argues that consensus revenue for FY2027 (plus 11.2 percent) and especially FY2028 (plus 7.5 percent) is conservative if capacity additions land as described.

  • Above consensus: data-centre demand "developing faster than management expected"; all data-centre factories at full throttle; Norlake "effectively doubles" US inductive capacity, to be filled with AQ's own backlogged volumes; further capacity expansions necessary in 2027; data-centre and Finnish defence backlogs to be recovered in Q4 2026; defence nominations expected late 2026; grid projects building toward year-end; management believes it is taking share; the CEO's aim to double EPS again by 2031.
  • In line or cautious: truck orders declining sequentially; Mexico wiring still lagging; Time 24 behind expectations; semiconductor customers delaying; management attributing part of Q3's 10 percent margin to US and China utilisation offsetting European holidays; delivery precision "unacceptable".
  • Why the gap: management talks about capacity and demand it can see; brokers, with short order books and no guidance, model deceleration and exclude unannounced acquisitions.
C-73

What else is material or uncommon about the company's guidance or investor relations?

C55

Judgement: four uncommon features matter for how the market prices AQ.

  • No numeric short-term guidance by policy: this makes results surprises frequent and mostly positive, and makes the stock sensitive to single quarters.
  • Thin and uniform coverage: four visible brokers (DNB Carnegie, Nordea, Pareto, one restricted), all rated Hold with targets SEK 232 to 260 and FY2027 EPS of SEK 10.00 to 10.40; DNB Carnegie is the only house with detailed published research in the pool; FactSet shows a buy share of 33 percent, implying brokers beyond the visible four. A uniform Hold consensus means there is no sell-side champion to lean against a de-rating, and also little sell-side bear modelling (the Wisdom Library's "bear case lives in qualitative framing": the only quantified bear value, SEK 140, comes from the Risks report, not from a broker model).
  • Candour: management admits operational failures publicly and quickly (India, Time 24, delivery precision), which builds credibility.
  • Disclosure noise: inconsistent prior-year EPS figures across Q3 2026 documents; data-centre customer concentration disclosed only in a call answer; no expert-network evidence on site leadership in the research pool. Next report: 11 February 2027.

Sell-side estimates

C613 questions
C-6760C-6966C-7458

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Consensus is credible, rising broadly across every visible broker, and built on a record of profit beats; it forecasts continuation of AQ's compounding (revenue about 10 percent and EPS about 14 percent a year to FY2028) with a modest further margin gain and depressed cash conversion. The forecasts are neither aggressive nor cleared: they sit between the company's aspirational targets and its cyclical history.

For the Consumer, the sell-side work yields specific adjustments to test in the model rather than wholesale disagreement: the tax line is probably 1 to 2 points too low from FY2027 (Pillar II), worth 1.5 to 2.5 percent of EPS; FY2028 revenue growth of 7.5 percent may be conservative if 2027 capacity additions land and acquisitions resume (consensus contains none and lets net cash pile up toward roughly SEK 2bn); FY2028 EBIT margin of 10.4 percent needs continued mix gains and is the most exposed line in a downturn; FCF assumptions (0.5 to 0.9 times net income) are appropriately cautious. Earnings momentum is strong (Sell-side Momentum A) and genuine (sales, EBITDA and EPS all revised up), but there is no expectations cushion if the cycle turns.

C-67

How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

C60

Judgement: consensus forecasts continuation of AQ's historical compounding (revenue about 10 percent, EPS about 14 percent a year) with a modest further margin gain, a slightly higher tax rate and depressed cash conversion; the gap to history is credible given the change forces, with risks balanced: upside from data-centre capacity and unmodelled acquisitions, downside from the cyclical base, the restocking fade, tax and cash.

Line by line (FactSet median, four brokers; history from the block; growth on the brokers' basis):

  • Revenue: FY2026 SEK 10,226m (plus 12.7 percent), FY2027 SEK 11,367m (plus 11.2 percent), FY2028 SEK 12,216m (plus 7.5 percent); history FY2019-25 10.0 percent a year, organic about 5 percent a year over FY2023-25. FY2026 requires Q4 revenue of about SEK 2,726m, up about 17 percent (calculated), credible after Q3's plus 21.4 percent and stated backlog recovery. FY2027 implies organic growth of roughly 8 to 9 percent plus annualised acquisitions (calculated estimate); credible. FY2028's 7.5 percent looks conservative if 2027 capacity lands and acquisitions resume, realistic if the data-centre line plateaus.
  • Margins: EBITDA (brokers' basis) 12.9 percent (FY2025) to 13.4, 13.7, 13.9 percent; EBIT 9.3 percent to 9.8, 10.1, 10.4 percent (history peak 9.6 percent in FY2024). Implied incremental EBIT margins 14.3, 12.6, 13.7 percent (calculated) against about 15 percent observed in 2026: credible. FY2028's 10.4 percent sits just above the "ceiling" named by both bull and bear research and needs continued mix gains.
  • D&A: SEK 349m, 394m, 421m (3.4 to 3.5 percent of sales) against 3.7 percent in FY2025: slightly light if capacity investment continues.
  • Below EBIT: net financial items about SEK minus 4 to minus 6m; tax implied about 20.3, 19.7 and 20.2 percent against 18.1 percent (FY2025): probably 1 to 2 points too low for FY2027-28 given Pillar II (EPS about 1.5 to 2.5 percent too high, calculated).
  • EPS: SEK 8.77, 10.15, 11.05 (plus 17.6, 15.8, 8.8 percent) against 17.2 percent a year historically; shares about 91.7m to 92.2m (warrant exercises).
  • Cash: operating cash flow SEK 858m, 964m, 1,260m; capex SEK 264m, 294m, 313m (about 2.6 percent of sales, matching history); FCF SEK 406m, 648m, 866m, 0.51, 0.70 and 0.86 times net income against 1.07 average in FY2023-25: credible and appropriately cautious given net working capital at about 30 percent of sales.
  • Balance sheet and returns: equity SEK 5,316m to 7,804m; book value per share SEK 59.15 to 85.00; DPS SEK 2.12, 2.37, 2.60 (about 24 percent payout). FCF after dividends implies net cash rising toward roughly SEK 2bn by FY2028 absent acquisitions (calculated), which would further dilute ROE; consensus contains no use for that cash.
  • Segments (FactSet): Component SEK 9,312m, 10,373m, 11,044m; System SEK 1,430m, 1,559m, 1,644m; group eliminations about SEK minus 0.9bn (a new line, so the Component figures are gross and not comparable with history).
C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

C66

Judgement: a strong record of beating estimates on profit, especially recently, against a weaker record on its own revenue ambitions; in comparable downturns (2020, 2024) earnings held up far better than revenue.

  • Last year: Q2 2026 revenue 1 percent above DNB Carnegie and consensus (SEK 2,577m against 2,554m), EBIT 6 percent above (SEK 255m against 240m), EBT margin 9.9 percent against 9.4 percent expected; Q3 2026 revenue 7.5 percent above Bloomberg consensus (SEK 2,566m against 2,387m) and 8.6 percent above DNB Carnegie, EBIT 16.7 percent above (SEK 259m against 222m), organic growth 17.9 percent against 9 percent expected. Weaker quarters a year earlier: Q3 2025 EBT margin 8.8 percent with about 0.7 points of mdexx dilution; Q4 2025 with quality provisions (DNB Carnegie's review title, "Focus on quality company over 'quality issues'", signals a disappointment). FY2025 EPS (brokers' basis SEK 7.46) landed mid-range of the year's consensus (SEK 7.20 to 7.66).
  • Three years: EBT margin above the 8 percent target for 13 to 14 consecutive quarters; total growth below the 15 percent target in 2024 and 2025; the SEK 10bn sales pledge for 2024 missed (SEK 8.55bn).
  • Five years: EPS doubled from FY2021 as targeted; 2022 saw delivery disruption and a margin dip (6.3 percent EBIT) but EPS still rose 12 percent.
  • Comparable conditions: in the 2024 truck downturn (organic minus 6.9 percent) EPS rose 5 percent and margin rose; in 2020 (Q2 organic minus 21 percent) every quarter stayed profitable. DNB Carnegie rates target credibility 5 out of 5.
  • Caveat: with no guidance there is nothing formal to miss; the beat record partly reflects conservative sell-side modelling in the absence of guidance.
C-74

To what extent have sell-side estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts, and has company guidance recently been rebased lower, or more aggressively kitchen-sinked or sandbagged?

C58

Judgement: not at all; estimates are being raised broadly and consistently, and nothing has been rebased or kitchen-sinked. This is an upgrade-momentum setup, not a cleared-expectations setup: it supports the shares while revisions continue but provides no cushion of low expectations if the cycle turns.

  • Breadth and direction (FY2027, FactSet): EPS plus 7 percent (one month), plus 9 (three), plus 12 (six), plus 11 (twelve); EBITDA plus 6, 7, 11, 9; sales plus 6, 7, 11, 10; target price plus 8, 9, 13. All four visible brokers updated on 15-16 October 2026 within a narrow FY2027 EPS range (SEK 10.00 to 10.40). Buy share unchanged at 33 percent; all visible ratings Hold. Dashboard Sell-side Momentum A; revision-momentum score 12 (level with Volex and second only to MTU's 13 in the 15-stock sector).
  • Earlier in the year: a mild trim phase (FY2026 consensus fell from about SEK 8.38 twelve months ago to about SEK 8.15 six months ago, with a low of SEK 7.94; DNB Carnegie cut FY2026 by 1 percent on tax in July), then upgrades after Q2 and Q3.
  • Company: no guidance to rebase; the margin target was raised, not cut; no kitchen-sink charges (the company reports no adjusted items); the only "sandbagging" is structural (refusal to guide).
  • Wisdom Library check ("composite signal masks component deterioration"): the components agree: EPS, EBITDA and sales revisions all rise with the target price, so the momentum is not propped up by target-price revisions alone; the target re-rating has run slightly ahead of earnings over six months (13 against 12 percent), a minor flag.

Stock market narrative

C601 question
C-2260

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The market's narrative for AQ is "a proven compounder that has become an AI-power and defence beneficiary", and the dated steps ahead mostly confirm that narrative rather than extend it. The near-term steps (Q4 backlog recovery, defence nominations, 2027 capacity, the first full year above 10 percent EBT) are well-founded and likely to arrive, but most are known; the novel steps (solid-state transformers, 800V DC, a step-up in acquisitions) are undated, exogenous or contrary to management's habits; and the narrative faces two dated negatives (harder comparisons from Q1 2027; Pillar II from 2027).

For the Consumer this means the operating model should be driven by the confirmed steps (capacity and backlog conversion in 2027; margin at about 10 percent; tax rising), and the novel steps should sit in scenarios, not in the base case. For the later sets, the narrative judgement is that AQ's share price will be driven less by new positive developments than by whether the AI-power narrative survives the current financing scare and whether organic growth decelerates gracefully from its Q3 2026 peak.

C-22

What staircase of novel positive developments lies ahead: which new, dated things can the market look forward to within the next 6 months, in months 6 to 18, and in months 18 to 36; how novel is each; and how well-founded?

C60

Judgement: a reasonable staircase, front-loaded with well-founded but already-narrated steps (Q4 backlog recovery, defence nominations, capacity additions, a first full year above 10 percent EBT), with the genuinely novel steps (solid-state transformer production, 800V DC orders, a step-up in acquisitions) undated or exogenous, and with two negative steps inside it (harder comparisons from Q1 2027; Pillar II tax from 2027).

Next 6 months (to April 2027):

  • FY2026 report, 11 February 2027: recovery of delayed data-centre and Finnish defence deliveries (equipment installed October 2026); test of implied Q4 revenue up about 17 percent and EBIT about SEK 267m (calculated from consensus); first full quarter of Norlake; operating cash flow normalisation; delivery-precision recovery; dividend proposal (consensus SEK 2.12, up about 18 percent). Novelty low-to-medium; well-founded (stated by management).
  • Defence nominations on large European RFPs (expected late 2026): novelty medium-high (size undisclosed); foundation medium (the timing has already slipped from "during or shortly after the summer").
  • Possible acquisitions funded from net cash: novelty low (known playbook); foundation medium (discipline can mean none).
  • Q1 2027 report (late April): the first quarter against a recovered base; organic growth likely decelerates from 17.9 percent, a potential negative step for a narrative priced on acceleration.

Months 6 to 18 (April 2027 to April 2028):

  • 2027 transformer capacity additions and Norlake fill toward USD 35m of sales: novelty medium; well-founded (management stated, customers' forecasts rising).
  • First full year above 10 percent EBT (FY2027, reported February 2028): novelty medium (no full year has exceeded 9.6 percent); foundation medium-high; could prompt a further target revision or capital markets day (plausible, unannounced).
  • Turnarounds: Time 24 to group productivity; mdexx toward 15 percent; India quality; Mexico and Plattsburgh volumes. Novelty low; foundation medium.
  • Pillar II additional tax payments (2027): a negative step.
  • Solid-state transformer commercial production (2027-2028): novelty high; foundation low-to-medium (prototypes delivered; customer and volumes undisclosed).

Months 18 to 36 (April 2028 to October 2029):

  • 800V DC data-centre orders from 2028 (DNB Carnegie): novelty high; foundation low (undated, unsized, and also the re-award threat).
  • Progress toward doubling EPS again by 2031: novelty low; foundation medium.
  • Defence programme ramps and Nordic grid investment (EUR 25bn to 2035): novelty low-to-medium; well-founded but slow.
  • Balance-sheet deployment (net cash rising toward roughly SEK 2bn on consensus by FY2028 if unused): novelty high if it happens; foundation low on management's record.

The staircase is good enough to support the earnings path (the framework treats a broadly understood staircase as normally sufficient), but it offers few genuinely new, dated surprises before 2028, and the largest steps are exogenous (hyperscaler capex, defence procurement timing, power-architecture choices).

Company: AQ Group AB (AQ-SE, Nasdaq Stockholm), a decentralised Swedish contract manufacturer of components and systems for industrial OEMs across 17 countries; Component segment (inductive components and transformers including medium-voltage units for data centres, wiring harnesses, sheet metal, injection moulding) and System segment (electrical cabinets and complete system assembly) serving commercial vehicles, construction and agricultural machinery, rail, defence, grid and data-centre power customers.

Request: T-P1

Peers: Kitron (Nordic contract manufacturer whose 2026 growth is defence-led), Scanfil (Nordic contract manufacturer expanding capacity into electrification), Note (Swedish contract manufacturer with slow 2026 organic growth), HANZA (Swedish contract manufacturer and acquirer with a history of low margins), Incap (Finnish contract manufacturer under Indian price pressure in 2026), Inission (Swedish contract manufacturer, a direct peer), Hammond Power Solutions (listed North American dry-type transformer maker exposed to data-centre demand), Tamura (Japanese transformer maker adding Mexican capacity), KB Components (Swedish component roll-up), SFS Group (Swiss component maker running a five-site network-streamlining programme), Bekaert (Belgian wire processor that closed 13 plants since 2019 to lift margins), Daetwyler (Swiss component maker running the ForwardNow cost programme) Inputs: What share of AQ Group's cost base is pass-through material, how much of personnel cost in its Bulgarian, Polish, Lithuanian and Estonian plants flexes with volume, and how large and fixed are site and head-office overheads? How did AQ Group's operating profit and margin move against revenue in 2020 (when Q2 2020 organic sales fell about 21 percent), in 2022 (input inflation), in 2023 and in the 2024 heavy-truck downturn, and why did profit hold when revenue fell? What explains the Q3 2026 result (company interim report, 15 October 2026: net sales up 21.4 percent, EBIT up 36 percent, EBT margin 10.0 percent), and how much did high utilisation in the US and China during European holidays contribute, as management stated? What does underutilisation cost at the Mexico and Plattsburgh, New York wiring plants and at the India inductive plant, and what actions and timelines exist to fill or resize them? How fast and at what cost have acquired units reached group margins (mdexx Magnetronics from about minus 7 to plus 7 percent EBT after a personnel-cost reduction; Michael Riedel Transformatorenbau; Time 24, acquired May 2026 and below expectations in Q3 2026; Norlake, acquired September 2026), and what does the Monitor ERP roll-out save? What do express freight, overtime and temporary labour cost when delivery precision falls (company KPI 91.1 percent in Q3 2026 against a 98 percent target), using the 2017, 2021 and 2022 episodes? What did SFS's network streamlining, Bekaert's plant closures and Daetwyler's ForwardNow programme deliver against what was promised, and over what time? What margin do medium-voltage data-centre transformers and custom inductive systems earn compared with wiring harnesses and sheet metal at AQ Group or comparable makers? Focus: Whether AQ Group's record of holding operating profit through revenue declines survives a volume-led European industrial recession now that more profit comes from capacity-constrained data-centre and defence lines, and how much of the 2026 margin step is temporary utilisation versus durable mix and cost.

Request: T-P2

Peers: Kitron (defence-weighted contract manufacturing pricing), Scanfil (contract manufacturer whose 2026 targets set margins at 7 to 8 percent), Note and Incap (contract manufacturers facing price competition), HANZA (Swedish contract manufacturer), Hammond Power Solutions (dry-type transformer maker selling into data centres), Tamura (transformer and inductor maker), Volex (data-centre cable assembler on short contracts), Huber+Suhner (designed-in connectivity supplier), Trelleborg (engineered-component maker that passed 2022 inflation through in full), SFS Group (custom cold-formed parts with OEM pass-through lags), Comer Industries (drivetrain supplier with indexed contracts and sole-supplier positions) Operating conditions: sharp copper and steel price spikes (2021-22; copper above USD 14,000 a tonne in May 2026; EU steel out-of-quota duties raised to 50 percent from 1 July 2026); a heavy-truck and agricultural-machinery downturn with spare capacity across Eastern Europe (2024); capacity-constrained data-centre power demand (2025-26); annual OEM productivity-cut negotiations; Swedish krona translation swings; US tariff escalation; technology re-award cycles as data centres move to 800V DC and solid-state transformers (2026-2028) Inputs: How do AQ Group's raw-material price-adjustment clauses work by customer group, what is the lag (DNB Carnegie cites three to six months), and how fully were 2021-22 cost increases recovered in 2023? What annual productivity price reductions do automotive, truck and industrial OEM customers demand of AQ Group and comparable suppliers, and how often are they conceded? Is there evidence of real price increases in medium-voltage data-centre transformers, given DNB Carnegie's October 2026 report that AQ Group is winning every US quote it submits? How do termination-for-convenience clauses and multi-year vehicle programmes affect price renegotiation? Request: T-P3

Why requested: AQ Group's next three years depend on interacting loops (data-centre capacity and design wins, a counter-cyclical cash-and-acquisition engine, and growth-driven working-capital and delivery strain) whose net direction decides whether its 2026 acceleration compounds or limits itself. Peers: Volex (data-centre growth accompanied by falling cash conversion and rising inventory), Halma (a data-centre-exposed unit valued below its core), Kitron and Scanfil (contract manufacturers adding capacity into defence and electrification), HANZA (acquisitive Nordic contract manufacturer), Lifco and Addtech (decentralised Swedish serial acquirers), Judges Scientific (small-deal serial acquirer), Hammond Power Solutions (transformer maker adding capacity for data centres), ABB (AQ Group's largest data-centre customer and route to hyperscalers) Inputs: A reinforcing loop in data centres: qualified design slots lead to capacity additions (Norlake doubling US inductive capacity, September 2026; further transformer capacity planned for 2027), which lead to more wins and manufacturing learning; how strong is it and what stops it? A reinforcing counter-cyclical loop: downturns release working capital, net cash builds (2025), cheap acquisitions follow (DNB Carnegie cites about 6 times EBITA), group volume fills acquired plants, margins and cash rise; does it still work as entry multiples change? A reinforcing share loop from nearshoring and local-for-local footprints across 17 countries. Balancing loops: rapid growth absorbing working capital (Q2 2026 operating cash flow SEK 23m against SEK 232m a year earlier, per the interim report); delivery precision and quality slipping during ramps (91.1 percent in Q3 2026; late-2025 transformer quality provisions), risking customer friction at re-award; capacity scarcity attracting competitor capacity (Tamura in Mexico; Scanfil's growth push), closing the window; OEMs capturing scale gains through productivity cuts; dependence on one data-centre customer's own success; managers stretched across turnarounds (Time 24, India, Norlake). What evidence shows each loop operating now, what precedents exist at the named peers, and what would strengthen, stall or reverse each? Focus: Which loop dominates in 2027-2028: the capacity-and-design-win loop in data centres plus the acquisition engine, or the balancing loops of working-capital absorption, delivery and quality strain, customer concentration and competitor capacity additions.

Request: T-P4

Why requested: Several inputs below operating profit and in capital allocation (the Pillar II tax step from 2027, unhedged currency translation on about 73 percent non-krona sales, working-capital intensity, acquisitions that consensus excludes, warrant dilution) move AQ Group's EPS and free cash flow materially and are thinly covered by its four visible brokers. Peers: Kitron, Scanfil, Note and HANZA (Nordic contract manufacturers with comparable tax and currency exposures), Trelleborg and Invisio (Swedish exporters that leave currency translation unhedged), SFS Group (Swiss component maker with franc translation drag), Lifco and Addtech (Swedish acquirers whose acquired growth is a modelled input), Volex (data-centre working-capital behaviour) Inputs: How far will AQ Group's effective tax rate rise under OECD Pillar II (reported tax rate 18.1 percent in FY2025; the CFO expects the first additional payments in 2027 because of profits in Bulgaria, Estonia and Lithuania), and what are the sell-side's assumptions? What currency translation sensitivity applies (2025 annual report: a 10 percent move changes sales by SEK 664m; a 5 percent EUR move changes profit before tax by SEK 17m), what do currency forecasters expect for the krona against EUR, PLN, CNY and USD, and why does AQ Group keep zero hedges when Note and Scanfil hedge? What normalised net working capital and inventory turns are achievable against the 3.5-turn target, and how did working capital behave in the 2021-22 upswing and the 2023-24 release? What acquisition cadence and acquired margins should be expected against the target of 5 percent acquired growth a year (Time 24, Gumboda, Norlake in 2026; DNB Carnegie's long-term scenario assumes 8 percent), and how quickly do acquired units converge to group margins? How will depreciation evolve with capacity investment (SEK 60m defence capacity in northern Sweden, Hungary, Wytheville, Norlake)? What dilution comes from the 2024/27 warrants (subscription price SEK 152.10) and the 2026/29 programme (up to 775,000 warrants at SEK 273)? What interest income does net cash earn and how would deployment change it? What revenue ramp is realistic for Norlake (management: up to USD 35m a year at full utilisation) and for the data-centre business (6 percent of group sales in Q2 2026)? How do management, the sell-side and the named peers' records differ on each of these inputs? Focus: How far the tax rate rises from 2027 and what normalised working capital and acquisition contribution look like, because these, more than operating margin, set the gap between consensus EPS and free cash flow and actual outcomes.

Layer 2

Translating the foundational components into an operating and financial model for the next 18 months, and 18 months to 3 years out

9 questions in 1 group · 7,593 words · written 25 October 2026, 18:10 to 18:27 UK, Opus, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years, based on the first set's answers, a base of the company's historical financial and operating performance, and how businesses in the sector and industry outperform or underperform, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a trustworthy judgement regarding the predictability of delivery of the financial forecasts over the next 18 months.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the likely effect on the company's financial forecasts is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes for this layer

The Investment Analyst's title
AQ Group (AQ-SE): answers to the second set of questions (financial forecasts)

Written 2026-10-25 for the Investment Analyst who answers the third and fourth sets. Money in SEK millions unless stated; "calculated" marks a figure I derived from the files. My base case is in the forecast-inputs form and summarised in P-10. Five framing points run through every answer:

  1. Basis. Company EBIT is after all amortisation and matches FactSet's annual adjusted EBIT (FY2025: 840 against 841). FactSet's quarterly EBIT and EBITDA lines understate the company's quarterly figures by SEK 10 to 19m a quarter (Q3 2026 FactSet EBIT 240 against company 259), so all quarterly margins below are company EBIT plus FactSet-derived D&A. Consensus EPS is on the brokers' basis (FY2025 SEK 7.46 against reported SEK 7.38); my EPS cross-check uses the same basis.
  2. The 2026 base is flattered by about 0.2 points. A SEK 22m gain on the Gävle property sits in 2026 interim reporting (most likely Q2). If it is inside EBIT, the underlying nine-month EBIT margin is about 9.6 percent, not 9.85 percent, and underlying FY2026 is about 9.65 percent (calculated). The first set called this item small; it is about a quarter of the margin step the market is extrapolating from 2026.
  3. AQ is two businesses. A base of about 86 percent of sales (harnesses, sheet metal, moulding, systems, conventional magnetics) at about 9 to 9.5 percent EBIT, and a structural slice of about 14 percent (data-centre magnetics about 6.5 percent, defence about 6 percent, grid) that I estimate earns 13 to 14 percent EBIT (segment margins are not disclosed). I model them separately (table in P-10).
  4. Q3 2026's 17.9 percent organic growth was mostly the base, not data centres. Data centres at about 7 percent of sales growing above 60 percent add about 2.6 points; defence about 1 point; so the base grew about 16 percent organically against a weak Q3 2025 (calculated). That makes the 2027 deceleration a base-business question first and a data-centre question second.
  5. My base case versus consensus: revenue in line for FY2026 and about 1 percent below for FY2027-28; EBIT margin in line for FY2026, 0.1 points below for FY2027 and 0.2 points below for FY2028; tax about 1 point above consensus from FY2027. EPS on the brokers' basis: SEK 8.80, 9.71, 10.70 against consensus 8.77, 10.15, 11.05. The FY2027-28 gap of 3 to 4 percent is mostly tax, then margin, then revenue.

Financial forecasts

C569 questions
P-0157P-0255P-0352P-0442P-0560P-0660P-0842P-0956P-1057

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

AQ's next four years are a decelerating compounding of a proven engine rather than a step change: revenue about 9 percent a year, EBIT margin edging from about 9.65 percent underlying to about 10.3 percent, EPS about 11 percent a year, and cash conversion improving as growth slows, with the forecasts for the next 18 months well supported on margin and data-centre volume and weakest on tax, working capital and the base business's comparisons from Q2 2027. The defining change from 2019-25 is that the amplifiers are gone: margin can rise only by mix and site fixes (the 10 percent level is near the industry ceiling and price adds nothing in real terms), tax turns from tailwind to headwind, and the balance sheet that could restore the gearing sits unused by habit.

The joined-up picture for the Consumer: (1) the data-centre loop is real, capacity-bound and strongest in 2027, but it is only 6 to 10 percent of sales and runs through one gatekeeper customer that appears to keep the pricing rent, so it lifts revenue and mix, not price; (2) the base business, not data centres, produced most of Q3 2026's 17.9 percent organic growth, so the size of the 2027 deceleration depends chiefly on a European industrial and truck cycle that is flat to fragile; (3) operating leverage is low on the way up and has historically been low on the way down, but new step-fixed capacity makes the next downturn more painful than 2020 or 2024; (4) the gap to consensus is small in EBIT and concentrated in tax, where consensus assumes the rate falls in 2027 despite Pillar II; (5) the company's EPS-doubling ambition for 2031 cannot be met organically and is effectively a statement about balance-sheet use. For predictability (the third set), the earnings cone for FY2027 is moderate (about minus 15 to plus 9 percent) and skewed down; for the fourth set, consensus FY2027-28 EPS is more likely to be trimmed for tax and comparisons than raised, after one or two more quarters in which reported numbers beat.

P-01

After assessing the fixed-cost operating leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, against the last four to six years?

C57

Judgement: fixed-cost operating leverage is modest and will shape the lines less than it did in 2023-26; from 2027 the EBIT margin will rise by mix and by the fill of new capacity, not by classic absorption, so incremental EBIT margins fall from about 15 percent (2026) to about 11 to 13 percent (2027-30), and the EBIT margin creeps from about 9.65 percent underlying in 2026 to about 10.3 percent by 2030. Against 2019-25, when the EBIT margin rose 2.9 points (6.4 to 9.3 percent) and EBIT compounded at 17.2 percent a year, the next four years give about 0.7 points of margin and EBIT growth of about 11 to 12 percent a year. EBIT will track revenue plus 1 to 2 points, not revenue plus 7.

Why the leverage is modest (the cost structure). Materials are about 48 percent of revenue (Q1 2026: SEK 1,126m on 2,358m) and pass through with a 3-to-6-month lag; personnel is the next block and about 65 percent of staff sit in Bulgaria, Poland, Lithuania and Estonia, where decentralised managers flex hours and headcount and 20 percent annual turnover gives attrition; head office is small; D&A is 3.5 to 3.7 percent of sales. AlphaSense found no quantified fixed/variable split for AQ; the peer read is the same model (Kitron direct materials about 65 percent of direct cost; Scanfil materials and bought services about 70 percent of operating costs; Incap 64 percent contract workers).

The history, read correctly. Incremental EBIT margins (calculated): FY2022 3.7 percent (inflation outran clauses); FY2023 17.9 percent (price catch-up); FY2024 EBIT up on revenue down 4.6 percent; FY2025 3.5 percent (mdexx dilution of about 0.7 points of EBT, Q4 quality provisions, currency); nine months 2026 15.3 percent. The 2019-25 average of about 13 percent hides that most of the margin gain came in two jumps (2023 price catch-up; 2024 cost cutting in a downturn), not from steady absorption.

Why 15 percent drop-through does not persist into 2027-30:

  • The 2026 rate is flattered: the Gävle gain (SEK 22m) and Q3's high US and China utilisation during European holidays (management and DNB Carnegie call it partly temporary). Q3 2026 revenue was flat on Q2 against a normal 10 to 13 percent seasonal fall, which is the clearest sign of non-repeating utilisation.
  • Acquired revenue arrives below group margin every year: at my 3 percent a year acquired growth, new units at about 4 to 6 percent margin cost about 0.05 to 0.1 points a year until they converge (12 to 24 months; mdexx took about 18 months to reach group margins, confirmed in Q3 2026). Time 24 lost SEK 5m after tax in Q3 2026; Norlake trails the group.
  • Capacity now comes in steps that carry fixed cost ahead of fill: Norlake (USD 7m of 2025 sales against USD 35m capacity), SEK 60m of defence capacity in northern Sweden, 2027 transformer lines, skilled winders that cannot be hired back quickly. This is new for AQ: the structural slice has step-fixed costs that the base never had.
  • D&A steps up: capex rises to about 3.0 percent of sales in 2027 (against 2.6 percent in 2025) and adds about SEK 40m of D&A a year.

Downside leverage is higher than history implies, and the first set understated this. The near-zero decremental margins of 2020 and 2024 rested on two cushions: metal-price deflation shrank revenue without shrinking value added, and AQ had little dedicated new capacity. In a volume-led downturn with copper near USD 14,000 a tonne there is no deflation cushion, and AQ would hoard scarce winders and keep data-centre lines ready. I estimate decremental margins of 15 to 20 percent on lost base revenue: a 7 percent fall in the base (the FY2024 precedent) costs about SEK 110 to 140m of EBIT, 10 to 12 percent of FY2027 EBIT (calculated). The 8 percent EBIT floor (the old target, never breached in 14 quarters) is the right deep-downturn floor.

How it shapes the lines (base case, calculated):

FY2019-25 FY2025 FY2026 FY2027 FY2028 FY2030
Revenue growth 10.0% a year +6.0% +12.8% +10.0% +8.0% +7.5%
EBIT margin (reported) 6.4% to 9.3% 9.3% 9.9% (9.65% ex gain) 10.0% 10.2% 10.3%
Incremental EBIT margin about 13% 3.5% 14.6% 11.3% (13.5% ex gain) 12.8% 11.0%
EBITDA margin 10.7% to 13.0% 13.0% 13.4% 13.55% 13.75% 13.85%

EBIT compounds at about 11.5 percent a year FY2025-30 (840 to 1,447) against 17.2 percent FY2019-25. The cone for FY2027 EBIT is about plus or minus 10 percent in ordinary scenarios (base revenue plus or minus 5 percent; data-centre revenue plus or minus 20 percent), with a fat lower tail (minus 20 to 25 percent) only in a synchronised European recession. The skew of EBIT surprise is up in the next two to three quarters (backlog catch-up in Q4 2026, the record of beats) and down from H2 2027 (harder comparisons, acquired dilution, competitor capacity in data-centre magnetics from late 2027).

P-02

After assessing the financial leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share and the balance sheet over the next 18 months to four years?

C55

Judgement: there is no financial leverage and the base case adds none, so financial leverage neither helps nor hurts EPS; its real effect is that net cash compounds to about SEK 2.4bn including leases by 2030 (about 1.25 times EBITDA), diluting ROE and leaving the company's own 2031 EPS-doubling ambition dependent on a balance-sheet decision management has never taken. This is the largest single lever between my base case and the bull case, and it is not in consensus either.

  • Starting point: net cash SEK 467m including leases at Q3 2026 (FactSet 468; cash 904, debt 436 of which leases about SEK 400m, calculated from the company's SEK 817m net cash excluding leases at Q2); equity ratio 67 to 68 percent against a 40 percent floor; no covenants, no hedges, no derivatives. Net financial items run at about SEK minus 1 to minus 2m a quarter (EBT less EBIT).
  • Path (base case, calculated): FCF after leases of SEK 495m, 620m, 835m, 915m, 955m (FY2026-30), less acquisitions of 130 to 195m a year and dividends of 24 percent of EPS, leaves net cash including leases of about SEK 0.6bn at end-2026, 0.9bn (2027), 1.3bn (2028), 1.9bn (2029) and 2.4bn (2030). Equity rises from about SEK 5.3bn to about 9bn; the equity ratio moves above 70 percent.
  • EPS effect in the base case: net financial items move from about minus 6 (2026) to about plus 18 (2030) as interest income on rising cash outgrows lease interest (my assumption: about 2 percent on cash). That is worth only about SEK 0.15 a share by 2030. ROE drifts down by roughly a point because equity grows faster than profit.
  • The latent lever: deploying SEK 2bn at AQ's historic about 6 times EBITA buys about SEK 330m of EBITA; net of lost interest and tax that is about SEK 2.5 a share, about 20 percent of FY2030 EPS (calculated). Re-gearing to 2 times EBITDA would raise capacity to about SEK 4.5 to 5bn by 2028. Nothing in management's record (no deals in 2025 on price; never a buyback; capital-structure target unchanged in July 2026) supports putting this in the base case.
  • The connection the Consumer should carry to the fourth set: the company targets doubling EPS by 2031 from the twelve months to Q3 2026 (reported SEK 8.22, calculated), that is about SEK 16.4. My base case reaches about SEK 13.6 in FY2031 (calculated, extending my FY2030 at 8 percent). The roughly 17 percent shortfall is about what SEK 3bn of deployment at 6 times EBITA would close (calculated). So the doubling ambition is credible only if management uses the balance sheet for larger or more frequent acquisitions; organic growth, margin and the current acquisition cadence do not get there, because the margin contribution that supplied about 40 percent of 2019-25 EPS growth is exhausted and tax is rising.
  • Risk side: leverage adds no risk; the counter-cyclical working-capital release (FY2024 operating cash flow SEK 1,197m) means a downturn would raise net cash further, which is the self-hedge that funds cheap acquisitions when targets are cheapest.
P-03

After assessing the scope for cost elimination and efficiency in greater detail, how much is likely to reach the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, and when?

C52

Judgement: there is no large cost-elimination programme to model; there is a set of site-level fixes worth about SEK 55 to 85m of EBIT by 2028 (0.5 to 0.7 points of margin), of which I expect only about 0.3 to 0.4 points to reach the reported margin, because OEM productivity price cuts, Eastern European wage inflation and newly acquired units absorb the rest. The larger efficiency prize is cash, not margin: inventory turns from 2.9 to about 3.2 would release roughly SEK 200m (estimate), but I put only a slow improvement in working capital in the base case because the KPI has missed its target for three years.
