The precedents are mixed: AQ's own mdexx took about 18 months to group margin; Jefferies and the IEA put typical US transformer plant ramps at two to three years because of engineering-talent shortages; Hammond's Monterrey 4 came on ahead of schedule but Hammond's backlog fell 6.9 percent sequentially in Q2 2026 as it converted faster. The model's 2027 data-centre growth of plus 50 percent is bounded by capacity, which management can see, and the demand one tier up is far ahead of it; the risk is pace and start-up margin, not demand. Dependence: moderate for 2027 revenue (about 3 points of the 10 percent), low for margin in 2027 (Norlake is small), higher for 2028.
  • Recovering delivery precision while ramping. 91.1 percent in Q3 2026 against 98 percent; the precedent is a fall to 90 percent in 2022 recovered to 94 percent by 2025 through capacity investment and 1,000 hires, that is, three years to recover four points and the target never reached. The forecast does not need 98 percent; it needs no customer loss, and the 2023 loss of a SEK 250m battery-system account over quality (a SEK 100m quarterly drag and five points of organic growth a year later) shows the cost of failure. Dependence: low for the base case, high for the tail.
  • Site leadership depth. Dan Day runs Virginia and Norlake; Rockford staff run Time 24; India's inductive leadership was replaced in Q3 2026. The second set's 12-to-24-month convergence assumption for acquired units already allows for this. Dependence: moderate, mostly on margin timing.
  • North American scale and a next-generation design-in. Hammond's Fort Worth site (CAD 250m of capacity from Q4 2027, up to CAD 400m), Tamura's Tijuana expansion (four times FY2023 capacity by FY2027) and EMG acquisition, Forgent's fivefold footprint and Eaton, Schneider and Siemens programmes arrive from late 2027; ABB's solid-state transformer units are expected in the second half of 2027, with native 800V DC estimated at 15 to 40 percent of 2030 new builds depending on the house. AQ has delivered prototypes and has no disclosed series award. These strengths are not needed inside the 18-month window; they govern whether the model's 2028-30 data-centre line (plus 20 percent, then plus 10 percent a year, margin easing from 14 to 13.3 percent) is right. The model has already assumed a halving of growth and a modest margin fade, which is a reasonable way to carry an unacquired strength without betting on it.
  • Working-capital improvement. The 3.5-turn target has been missed since it was set; turns are 2.9. The model (after my calibration) assumes a slow drift, not the target. Dependence: low for EPS, high for cash.

Peer precedents on whether this kind of transition succeeds. The plausibility report found no clean case of a build-to-print contract manufacturer moving to a sustained double-digit pre-tax margin, and the closest analogues cut both ways: Volex (10.2 percent underlying operating margin as data-centre sales doubled, but cash conversion down to 63.9 percent and a half-year update in October 2026 noting that share gains won when competitors were capacity-constrained were reversing as supply normalised); Halma's Avo Photonics (a single-customer data-centre ramp to 20 percent of group revenue, valued by Jefferies at 13 times EBITA against 19.4 times for the rest, and now facing Google's dual-sourcing); Kitron (defence-led, margins 9.5 percent, but a 2024 revenue fall of 16.5 percent when destocking hit); Incap (EBITA margin from a 13.4 percent historical median to 9.0 percent on Indian price competition); Tamura (guidance raised on data-centre demand but operating margin 4.5 percent against a 7 percent plan). The pattern is that the margin level is attainable while capacity is scarce, that it is tested when capacity arrives (2027-29 here), and that the cash and concentration costs of the ramp are the reliable part of the precedent. AQ differs from these cases in two helpful ways: its growth niche is about 7 percent of sales inside a diversified base, so a Volex-type reversal costs it far less; and it has a thirty-year record of defending margin in downturns that none of the peers match.

Weighing it. The 18-month forecast asks the business to keep doing what it did in Q2 and Q3 2026 at a slightly lower growth rate, to fill capacity it already owns with demand it can already see, and to absorb dilution from small acquisitions it has already made. All of that is demonstrated. It does not ask for pricing power, for a leverage decision, for the 3.5-turn target or for a design win. The weakest link inside the window is the quarter-by-quarter sustainability of a 10 percent margin while delivery precision is below 92 percent and the organisation is stretched; the record says such episodes are fixed within two to four quarters, and the model's margin path gives up most of the Q3 utilisation benefit anyway. Beyond the window, plausibility falls because the capacity-scarcity rent ends and the technology re-award begins, and the model's 2028-30 fade is an honest but unverifiable way to handle it.

D-03

In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?

C52

Judgement: the drivers are moderately concentrated and more concentrated than in 2019-25, but in a way that helps rather than hurts 18-month predictability. About two-thirds of the forecast's organic growth comes from a structural slice that is 14 percent of sales, and almost half of it from one product line (data-centre magnetics) that reaches hyperscalers largely through one customer (ABB); but that line is capacity-bound for the next 18 months, which makes it the most predictable large driver in the model. The genuinely diffuse and uncertain driver is the European industrial base, and the hidden common factor that links the two engines is the cost and availability of finance.

What differs between the forecast and history, and what generates each difference (calculated from the model and the block).

Line FY2019-25 history Forecast FY2026-30 What generates the difference Concentration
Revenue growth 10.0% a year; price-led in 2022-23; organic about 5% a year 2023-25; acquisitions about 3 to 4% a year 9.1% a year; organic about 11.5, 7.5, 5.5, 4.5, 4.5%; acquired about 3% Structural slice (data centres about 3.2 points of 2027 organic growth, defence about 1.5, grid 0.1) against base 2.6 points; acquisitions carry 3 of the 10 points Concentrated: 65% of 2027 organic growth from 14% of sales; 43% from one line
EBIT margin 6.4% to 9.3%; the rise came in two jumps (2023 price catch-up; 2024 cost cuts) 9.9%, 10.0, 10.2, 10.25, 10.3% Mix (slice at about 14% margin growing to 19.5% of sales adds about 0.5 points by 2030), site fixes (Time 24, Norlake, India, delivery costs: about 0.3 to 0.4 points net), offset by acquired dilution and wage inflation Concentrated in the same slice for the mix part; diffuse across 40 companies for the site part
EPS growth 17.2% a year, 40% of it from margin, with a falling tax rate 10.9% a year (model about 10.4% after FY2026) EBIT growth about 11 to 12% a year less a tax drag of about 1 point a year; no share count change Tax is one mechanism (Pillar II in three jurisdictions)
Cash conversion 83% on FactSet's basis, from minus 34% to 157% by year 0.5 to 0.6 in FY2026 rising to about 0.75 (model, after calibration) Working capital at about 30% of sales absorbing 20 to 30% of incremental revenue; capex at 2.7 to 3.0% of sales Diffuse operationally (inventory sits in 40 companies) but one target metric (turns 2.9 against 3.5)
Net cash minus 0.1bn to plus 0.4bn about 0.5bn (2026) to about 1.85bn (2030) Free cash flow after dividends and 3% a year of acquisitions Depends on one management decision not taken

How concentrated the growth drivers are, and how correlated.

  • Data-centre magnetics (about 7 percent of 2026 sales; 43 percent of 2027 organic growth). Customers: ABB about 9 percent of group sales and the route to hyperscale deployments (HiPerGuard medium-voltage UPS; Applied Digital campuses), plus five to six other data-centre customers and a growing US direct base. End demand: hyperscaler and neocloud capital spending (Morgan Stanley's USD 1.6tn of AI capex in 2027; US data-centre power demand doubling to 66 GW by 2027 on Goldman Sachs' estimate), gated by grid connections (a 57 GW shortfall through 2028) and permitting (294 municipal moratorium resolutions in the first half of fiscal 2026; the Texas pause; a proposed Virginia moratorium bill from July 2027). Supply: AQ's own capacity (Virginia full; Norlake to fill; 2027 lines). For 18 months this driver's variance is bounded on the upside by capacity and on the downside by a backlog that management says is ahead of expectations; the model's plus 50 percent for 2027 is a capacity number more than a demand number. A hyperscaler capex pause would reach AQ within about two quarters (Risks report) and would first show as deferrals, not cancellations.
  • Defence (about 6 percent of sales; about 20 percent of 2027 organic growth). Government-anchored, multi-year, but lumpy in invoicing (2025 sales up 18 percent and still a disappointment; Q3 2026 deliveries slipped to Q4 on customer equipment delays); Germany's 2027 contract authorisations fall 26 percent and Italy scaled back its increase, so the European procurement ramp is slower than budgets imply. Kitron's defence revenue more than doubled to 46 percent of its sales in Q3 2026, which shows the demand exists and that AQ's share of it is small.
  • The European and North American industrial base (86 percent of sales; about 35 percent of 2027 organic growth; the whole of the downside tail). Diffuse by product (harnesses, sheet metal, moulding, systems, conventional magnetics) and by end market (trucks, buses, construction, agriculture, rail, medtech, packaging, marine), but concentrated by customer at the top (Volvo and ABB together about 20 percent of sales in 2023; top ten about half) and by cycle (correlation of organic growth with the European Business Climate Index about 0.65, per DNB Carnegie). Its 2027 path is the least predictable large input: the OEM schedules (Europe plus 3 percent, North America plus 13 percent in heavy trucks; construction orders plus 16 percent; agriculture at the bottom; rail backlog of five years) point to a decent first half, while dealer inventories (3.3 months against 2.4 normal), September Class 8 orders (minus 7 percent) and Bernstein's lower 293,000-unit forecast point to a destocking second half.
  • Acquired growth (3 of the 10 points in 2027). Concentrated in one decision-maker's discipline and the seller-multiple environment; evidenced cadence 2 to 4 percent a year; management now says explicitly it wants more deals.
  • Tax. One mechanism (Pillar II top-up on Bulgarian, Estonian and Lithuanian profit, with substance carve-outs declining from 9.6 and 7.6 percent in 2025 toward 5 percent in 2033). Highly predictable in direction, uncertain by about one point in level until the FY2026 tax note.
  • Working capital. Diffuse operationally; concentrated in one KPI the company publishes quarterly.

The common factor. The two engines look independent (AI power and European trucks) but share financing conditions: the US 10-year near 5.1 percent, a priced December Federal Reserve hike and widening neocloud credit spreads bear on hyperscaler and neocloud build schedules at the same time as they bear on European OEMs' customers (truck fleets deferring on energy-driven inflation and rates). This is what gives the operating cone its downside skew (D-04): the bear case is not two independent shocks but one.

Compared with history and peers. In 2019-25 the growth came from a broad cyclical base, a price surge in 2022-23 and a run of acquisitions; no single line was more than about a quarter of the growth. The forecast's concentration is higher, but the concentrated driver is the one whose next 18 months is best underwritten (capacity-bound, demand visibly ahead, orders confirmed one tier up), so concentration raises predictability over the window and lowers it beyond (capacity arrivals from late 2027; re-award from 2027-28). Relative to peers the concentration is modest: Kitron has 46 percent of sales in defence; Volex's data-centre growth rests on Amazon and Nvidia under a consignment-hub model; Halma's Avo rests on one hyperscaler; Hammond and Tamura are pure transformer plays. AQ's structural slice at about 14 percent of sales, split across data centres, defence and grid and across several customers, is a narrower bet than its narrative implies in either direction: it cannot carry the group if the base falls, and its stall cannot sink the group.

Sensitivities for the Consumer (calculated, FY2027). Data-centre revenue plus or minus 20 percent moves EBIT by about SEK 35 to 45m (3 to 4 percent of FY2027 EBITA). Base revenue plus or minus 5 percent at a 15 to 20 percent drop-through moves EBIT by SEK 70 to 95m (6 to 8 percent). Tax plus or minus 1 point moves EPS by about 1.3 percent. Acquired growth plus or minus 1.5 points moves revenue by SEK 170m and EBIT by SEK 10 to 15m in year one. Working capital plus or minus 1 point of sales moves free cash flow by about SEK 110m. The ranking (base, then data centres, then tax, then acquisitions) is the opposite of the narrative's ranking, and it is why the organic growth rate of the base in Q2 and Q3 2027 is the number to watch.

D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

C55

Judgement: the cone is narrow for the next two reports, moderate for FY2027 (EPS about minus 15 to plus 10 percent around the model's SEK 9.78, revenue plus 4 to plus 14 percent, EBIT SEK 1,000m to 1,240m) and wide for cash (free cash flow SEK 350m to 750m); it is skewed up for the next six months and down for the 18 months as a whole, because the bear case joins two shocks that share one cause while the bull case is capped by capacity. The case is therefore predictable on earnings to a degree unusual for a contract manufacturer, and unpredictable on cash and on the share price's reaction to a deceleration that is itself predictable.

The cone by horizon.

  • FY2026 (one report left, 11 February 2027): nine months are known (revenue SEK 7,501m, EBIT SEK 739m, EPS SEK 6.38). The model's year (SEK 10,232m, EBITA 1,019m, EPS 8.86) needs a Q4 of about SEK 2,730m revenue and SEK 270m EBIT. Range: revenue SEK 2,650m to 2,800m (plus 14 to plus 20 percent; the low end if defence and data-centre catch-up slips again, the high end if it lands fully and Norlake consolidates); EBIT SEK 240m to 290m (the low end if Q4 again carries quality or inventory provisions, as Q4 2025 did and as 2025's SEK 185m of inventory write-downs show is possible; the high end if Q3's utilisation persists). EPS SEK 8.6 to 9.1 on the model's basis, a cone of about plus or minus 3 percent. Skew: up on revenue, balanced on EBIT because Q4 is AQ's clean-up quarter.
  • FY2027: Scenarios, with my probabilities (the second set's were 20/60/20; I move weight to the tails on both sides because of the macro regime and the base business's strength):
  • Bear (25 percent): a European industrial recession from mid-2027 (truck production cut as dealer inventories of 3.3 months unwind; agriculture stays at the bottom; European short-cycle demand stays below its 2021 peak) coincides with a hyperscaler or neocloud financing squeeze that defers data-centre deliveries by two to three quarters. Revenue plus 3 percent (base minus 4 percent organic, data centres plus 20 percent, acquisitions plus 3), EBIT margin 9.0 percent as decremental margins of 15 to 20 percent bite and expediting costs persist, EPS about SEK 8.2 (minus 16 percent); free cash flow rises toward SEK 700m as working capital releases, which is the historically reliable offset (2023: SEK 980m of operating cash flow after the 2021-22 build).
  • Base (55 percent): the model as it stands, EPS SEK 9.78.
  • Bull (20 percent): the base keeps growing 6 to 8 percent through the first half on share gains and North American strength, data centres grow 60 to 70 percent as Norlake fills faster, acquisitions add 4 to 5 percent, margin 10.4 to 10.5 percent as mix and Time 24's break-even arrive together; EPS about SEK 10.7 (plus 9 to 10 percent). A deal of SEK 500m or more of enterprise value would add a further 2 to 4 percent and sits outside this scenario.
  • Probability-weighted EPS about SEK 9.6, 2 percent below the model and about 5 percent below consensus (calculated).
  • The 18-month column (to 31 March 2028): adds Q1 2028 (model plus 7.5 percent, margin 13.6 percent EBITDA). Over the full window the cumulative cone on EBIT is about minus 15 to plus 8 percent; the model's twelve-month EPS to Q1 2028 of about SEK 10.0 has a plausible range of SEK 8.4 to 10.9.

Line by line.

  • Revenue: the widest input is the base (plus or minus 5 points of organic growth, worth about SEK 475m); data centres plus or minus 20 percent (SEK 200m); acquisitions zero to plus 5 percent (SEK 0 to 560m); currency plus or minus 3 percent of sales (SEK 300m; 73 percent of sales is non-SEK and unhedged, a 10 percent move is SEK 664m). The acquisitions term is the only one skewed up.
  • EBIT margin: 9.0 to 10.5 percent; most of the downside is operating deleverage in the base, most of the upside is mix and site fixes landing early; price is plus or minus 0.3 points only from 2028.
  • Tax: 20 to 22 percent, with the FY2026 annual report's tax note (week 13 of 2027) the resolving event; a two-point miss is 2.6 percent of EPS.
  • Free cash flow: SEK 350m to 750m for FY2027 against about SEK 565m (my calibrated model). This is the widest line in proportion because working capital can swing by a point of sales (SEK 110m) in either direction on timing alone (Q2 2026's SEK 23m of operating cash flow followed by Q3's SEK 269m) and because the FY2027 cash-tax step (SEK 20 to 40m above the P&L charge) is not in the model. The counter-cyclical release means the cash cone is inversely skewed to the earnings cone: the bear case for earnings is the bull case for cash.

What the peer dispersion says about whether this cone is wide enough. The driver-concentration report assembled the post-peak record: Kitron's revenue fell 16.5 percent in 2024 while its EBIT margin held at 7.4 percent after a 20 percent headcount cut; Comer's revenue fell 23 percent with EBITDA margin flat at 16.7 percent after an 18.6 percent cut in direct labour; Scanfil's energy segment fell 28.4 percent in one quarter of destocking; Inission's margin fell to 5.4 percent because cuts lagged; Volex's share gains reversed within a year as competitor capacity normalised; Halma's Avo faces dual-sourcing after a two-year ramp; the 2011 telecom and 2012 component cycles show eight-quarter ramps flattening abruptly on inventory gluts. The lesson is that revenue cones after a growth peak are wide (minus 15 to minus 25 percent at the segment or company level) but margin cones for well-run contract manufacturers are far narrower, because labour is the flex. AQ's own 2020 and 2024 record is the strongest in the set (EBIT flat and up respectively). My bear case (revenue plus 3 percent, margin 9.0 percent) is consistent with that history; a Kitron-2024-style revenue fall for the group as a whole is a 5 to 10 percent probability tail, not the bear case, because AQ's base is diversified across end markets whose cycles are out of phase (agriculture at the bottom, trucks mid-recovery, rail at a backlog peak, data centres accelerating).

What would widen the cone (triggers, in order of plausibility over 18 months). A hyperscaler capital-spending guidance cut in early 2027 (transmission within two quarters; also a narrative break); a European truck production cut announced with Volvo's or Traton's Q4 2026 reports; a redesign or dual-sourcing decision at the ABB account (ABB's Specialtrasfo purchase sits in Motion, not Electrification, so the near-term risk is low, but ABB's own margin may have peaked in 2025 per Morgan Stanley, which raises its incentive to squeeze suppliers); a quality event in medium-voltage transformers (it happened in late 2025); a copper or steel spike with the three-to-six-month lag (0.2 to 0.4 points for one to two quarters); a krona rally (Morgan Stanley is bullish the krona). What would narrow it: a fourth quarter that converts the backlog cleanly, delivery precision back above 94 percent by Q1 2027, and an FY2026 tax note confirming a rate near 20 percent.

The skew, and why it is time-dependent. For the next two reports the skew is up: the record of beats (Q2 2026 EBIT 6 percent above, Q3 16.7 percent above DNB Carnegie), stated backlog recovery, a clean base (no Gävle flattery to lap) and a first quarter 2027 against a currency-depressed Q1 2026. From the second quarter of 2027 the skew turns down: comparisons harden against a base that grew 12 percent organically, acquired units dilute, the cash-tax step lands, competitor capacity is announced ahead of arriving, and the two engines' shared sensitivity to financing conditions means the downside scenarios compound. The second set's "alarm" of organic growth below 10 percent for two quarters is reached in the base case around Q2 or Q3 2027, so the market will see a deceleration that is predictable in the forecasts but may not be priced as such.

So how predictable is the case? On EBIT over 18 months, more predictable than the average company in this sector: a cone of roughly minus 15 to plus 8 percent, a margin that has never fallen below 8 percent in 14 quarters, a growth engine whose next 18 months are capacity-bound, and 55 percent of weight on a base case that sits below consensus. On cash, poorly predictable in any single year and well predictable cumulatively (the release follows the build). On the share price, the least predictable of the three, because the multiple will react to the direction of the organic growth rate, which is scheduled to turn down, and to the AI-financing narrative, which AQ does not control (D-07).

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

C50

Judgement: moderately helpful, and better than its peers' on candour and operating KPIs, but structurally unhelpful on the two numbers the thesis most needs, the data-centre line's revenue and margin and the order backlog, and thin in frequency over the next six months, when there are only two reports. The case can be monitored well, but mostly from outside the company: ABB's orders, the truck makers' schedules, the Nordic peers' reports and the hyperscalers' capital-spending guidance all arrive before or alongside AQ's own prints and transmit with lags that are known.

What the company discloses, and how directly each item monitors a driver.

  • Growth decomposition every quarter (organic, acquired, currency): directly monitors the deceleration path (model: plus 17.5, plus 16.2, plus 9.8 percent reported in the next three quarters; organic about 15, 12 to 13 and 6 to 7 percent, calculated). This is the single most useful disclosure, and it lets the Consumer tell a base-business slowdown from a structural one when combined with management's product-area commentary.
  • Operating KPIs every quarter (delivery precision 91.1 percent against 98; inventory turns 2.9 against 3.5; quality 99.6 against 100): directly monitor the execution strain and the cash thesis. Few peers publish all three. The useful thresholds: delivery precision back above 93 to 94 percent by Q1 2027 says the 2022-type recovery is under way; turns below 2.9 says the inventory build has become structural.
  • Qualitative product-area commentary with some numbers (data-centre inductive share about 6 percent of sales in Q2 2026; growth above 60 percent in Q3; 70 transformer systems a month; Virginia about USD 50m and full; Norlake USD 7m toward USD 35m; defence capacity SEK 60m; the named problem sites Mexico, Plattsburgh, India, Time 24): monitors the slice's momentum in direction, not in level, because the share and growth figures appear irregularly and only in calls or presentations.
  • Cash flow and balance sheet every quarter (operating cash flow, net cash SEK 467m including leases, equity ratio 67 to 68 percent): monitors the working-capital release directly.
  • Tax rate every quarter (20 percent for nine months 2026 against 18 percent a year earlier, "includes provisions for additional tax"): monitors the Pillar II accrual; the quantification comes only in the annual report's tax note (week 13 of 2027), which is the resolving document for the FY2027-28 tax line.
  • Candour: management names failures (delivery precision "unacceptable"; India leadership replaced; Time 24 behind plan; the Q2 cash drain explained as late-June invoicing; the slow pace of acquisitions listed as a "lowlight" of Q3 2026 with an explicit wish to do more deals). This makes the qualitative commentary trustworthy as a leading indicator in both directions.

What it withholds, and the cost of each gap to monitoring.

  • No segment or product-area margins: the mix claim behind the 10 percent target cannot be verified; the Consumer must infer the slice's margin from the group margin and the growth split. This is the largest gap because the whole margin thesis rests on mix.
  • No data-centre revenue line and no customer split: the 6 percent share and the 60 percent growth are call disclosures; the move from "one customer about half" to "several customers" between July and October 2026 was also a call answer. Nothing in the reported segments (Component about 85 percent, System about 15 percent) separates the drivers.
  • No order intake, backlog or book-to-bill: Kitron (record backlog EUR 843m), Scanfil (EUR 172m of new projects year to date), Note (first-half 2027 order book up 20 percent) and Hammond (backlog up 122 percent in 2025) all give one; AQ says order books are short and gives none. The only forward-looking numbers are capacity statements, which bound the slice's upside, and informal remarks about customers' forecasts.
  • No numeric near-term guidance, by policy: this is the mechanism behind the beat record but it also means every print is a surprise in one direction or the other, and the Consumer cannot tell before the report whether the quarter met the company's own plan.
  • Presentation changes ahead: IFRS 18 applies from 1 January 2027 with 2026 comparatives restated, so the Q1 2027 report (22 April 2027) will re-label management performance measures; the operating KPIs and the growth split should survive, but historical comparisons of subtotals need care.
  • Disclosure noise: the two different prior-year Q3 EPS figures in the Q3 2026 materials (one of which, SEK 2.06, was the Q2 2025 figure with the Gävle gain) and the Gävle gain's timing, which two sets of our own work mis-read, show that the interim documents require checking against the annual report.

The six-month monitoring calendar and what each event resolves. (1) Late January to early February 2027: Microsoft, Alphabet, Amazon and Meta report and guide 2027 capital spending; CoreWeave and other neoclouds' funding conditions (spreads widened about 300 basis points since late August 2026) will be visible; ABB's Q4 2026 report (electrification orders, data-centre book-to-bill after 1.41 in Q3) is the best single external indicator of AQ's 2027 data-centre line two quarters ahead. (2) Early February: Volvo, Traton and Daimler Truck Q4 reports and firm 2027 production outlooks; Volvo Construction Equipment orders; AGCO and Deere on the agricultural trough; Alstom's order intake. (3) 11 February 2027: AQ's FY2026 report: Q4 revenue against about SEK 2,730m and EBIT against about SEK 270m on the company basis; organic growth (about 15 percent expected); whether Q4 carries provisions; operating cash flow (about SEK 170m on my calibration, consensus about SEK 227m); delivery precision; the dividend proposal (model SEK 2.10); any statement on 2027 capacity additions, Norlake's run-rate and the acquisition pipeline; any first word from the new chair on capital structure. (4) Late January to late February: Kitron, Scanfil, Note, HANZA, Incap and Inission Q4 reports give the Nordic base-business read-through and the component-supply picture (analog lead times of 60 to 70 weeks). (5) Week 13 of 2027: the annual report, with the tax note (Pillar II accrual and the three jurisdictions' carve-outs), the country revenue and employee tables, the related-party and warrant details, and the audited working capital, which resolves the FY2026 cash reconciliation above. (6) 22 April 2027: Q1 2027 report (the first against a recovered base; organic growth about 12 to 13 percent expected, so a print below 10 percent would be an early thesis warning rather than the expected mid-year deceleration) and the AGM (board, remuneration committee composition, any new authorisation). Also continuous: LME copper and European steel prices (the three-to-six-month lag means a spike in Q4 2026 shows in Q2 2027 margins); the krona against the euro and dollar; US data-centre permitting news in Texas and Virginia; Hammond's and Tamura's capacity commissioning statements (not a six-month item but worth tracking for the narrative).

Recalibrating the memo's alarm levels for this window. The second set's organic-growth alarm (below 10 percent for two quarters) is expected to trigger in Q2 or Q3 2027 in the base case and so is not an alarm over the next six months; the alarms that would matter before April 2027 are: Q4 2026 organic growth below 12 percent (would say the base is decelerating a quarter early); a sequential fall in the data-centre share of sales or any statement that deliveries are being deferred by customers rather than by AQ's own capacity; delivery precision not improving from 91.1 percent for a second quarter; Q4 operating cash flow below SEK 100m; a Q4 provision of more than SEK 30m; a reported tax rate above 21 percent for FY2026; Norlake not consolidating at a run-rate above USD 10m annualised by Q1 2027; and, on the positive side, any acquisition above SEK 300m of enterprise value or a stated leverage framework, either of which would change the fourth-set picture.

Net. The company tells the Consumer, candidly and quarterly, how fast it is growing, where the problems are and how its working capital behaves; it does not tell the Consumer how profitable the thesis's engine is or how much it has booked. The external chain compensates well for the next six months because the engine's demand is confirmed one tier up and the base's demand one tier down, both with disclosed numbers. The gap that cannot be closed from outside is the margin mix, which is why the FY2026 report's group margin, not its growth, is the number that tests the thesis.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

C56

Judgement: the company's statements trace clearly in direction and cleanly through to EPS, because AQ reports no adjusted measures, has no leverage, a flat share count and a tax rate with one known driver; they trace poorly in magnitude at the one step that matters most, the conversion of structural-slice volume into group margin, because the company gives capacity in units and dollars but never the margin of what the capacity makes. The external transmission is unusually clear for a sub-supplier: each value-chain link publishes a number that leads AQ's by one to two quarters, and the lags are known.

From what the company says to the lines (the internal chain).

  • "EBT margin above 10 percent through the cycle" traces directly onto the EBIT and pre-tax lines (net financial items are about SEK minus 1 to minus 2m a quarter, so EBT and EBIT margins differ by under 0.1 point). The model has 9.8 percent for FY2026, 9.96 for FY2027 and 10.2 for FY2028. How the company says it gets there: more design responsibility and technology content (inductive components from 6 percent of revenue in 2018 to 25 percent in 2025), cost control and low fixed costs; what it does not say: the margin of inductive, data-centre or defence products, so the mix arithmetic (slice at an estimated 14 percent on 17 percent of 2027 sales, base at 9.2 percent) is ours, not theirs. The milestone that would confirm the chain is simple to read: a full-year EBT above 10 percent for FY2027, reported in February 2028.
  • "Sales growth above 15 percent a year, 10 organic and 5 acquired" traces onto revenue but has not been delivered (minus 4.6 percent in 2024, plus 6.0 in 2025, about plus 12.8 in 2026); consensus and the model use 10 to 11 percent for 2027. The company's capacity statements are the usable building blocks: Virginia at about USD 50m and full, Norlake from USD 7m toward USD 35m, "further transformer capacity expansions in 2027", SEK 60m of defence capacity in northern Sweden, 70 medium-voltage systems a month. Together they bound the structural slice's 2027 revenue at roughly SEK 1.9 to 2.1bn (calculated from the capacity figures at about SEK 9.5 to the dollar), which is where the model sits, and they say the slice's upside beyond that in 2027 is a capacity question, not a demand one.
  • "Double EPS every five years" (SEK 8.22 reported in the twelve months to Q3 2026 to about SEK 16.4 by 2031) traces onto EPS and, by subtraction, onto a balance-sheet decision: the model reaches about SEK 13.5 by 2031 (calculated), so the ambition is a statement that about SEK 3bn will be deployed at about 6 times EBITA, which management has now half-said ("we want to execute more deals"; the equity ratio of 67 to 68 percent against a 40 percent floor; DNB Carnegie's scenario of 8 percent acquired growth a year). The milestone is a deal of a different size, or a leverage framework from the new chair.
  • Operating KPIs (98 percent delivery, 100 percent quality, 3.5 inventory turns) trace onto cost (expediting, overtime, temporary labour, warranty: perhaps SEK 10 to 20m of EBIT, never quantified by the company) and onto working capital (each 0.1 of a turn on about SEK 1.7bn of inventory is about SEK 55m of cash, calculated). The incentive weights (80 percent of short-term bonus on EBT, 20 percent on inventory turns) say which the organisation will chase.
  • Equity ratio above 40 percent and a dividend of about 25 percent of cycle-average profit trace onto the balance sheet and the DPS line (model SEK 2.10 for FY2026, consensus 2.12); both are trivially predictable.
  • Through to EPS: EBIT less about SEK 5m of net financial items, taxed at about 20 to 21 percent, over 91.7m basic shares, less about SEK 5 to 7m of minorities that the model does not deduct (about 0.7 percent of EPS). No adjusted items, no capitalised development, no buybacks, acquired-intangible amortisation of about SEK 10 to 18m a year that the brokers add back. One SEK of EBIT margin point is about SEK 0.95 of EPS (calculated); one point of tax is about SEK 0.13 of EPS. Few companies trace this cleanly from operating profit to the per-share line.

The weak link in the internal chain. The thesis says the structural slice earns more than the base; the company neither confirms nor denies it, and the Risks and Narrative reports both identify segment margins as the first information gap. There is indirect evidence that it is true (a group margin that rose from 9.0 percent in Q3 2025 to 10.1 percent in Q3 2026 while the slice grew from about 10 to 14 percent of sales and the base was still absorbing Time 24's losses and Mexico's underutilisation; management's statement that higher technology content justifies the raised target), and indirect evidence of a limit (the CEO's remark that gross margins have not improved structurally and that the gains come from overhead absorption; pricing at or below inflation; the Hammond and Tamura price increases that AQ does not report capturing, consistent with ABB holding the pricing rent on medium-voltage UPS magnetics). The Consumer should treat the slice's margin as an inference of 12 to 15 percent, not a fact, and treat the group margin print as the test.

The external transmission (value chain to EPS), link by link with lags.

  • Hyperscaler and neocloud capital spending (guided in late January, visible in credit spreads continuously) to ABB Electrification orders and data-centre book-to-bill (ABB's Q3 2026: orders about USD 7.7bn, up 70 percent organically; data-centre orders up triple digits; book-to-bill 1.41; the Applied Digital campuses on HiPerGuard) to AQ's inductive and enclosure deliveries with a lag of roughly two quarters (Risks report) to revenue and mix. Direction clear; magnitude capped by AQ's capacity; the one break in the chain is that ABB could redirect (dual-source, white-label local assemblers, or extend Specialtrasfo beyond Motion), on which the ABB sourcing report finds low near-term risk and medium beyond 2027.
  • Truck and machinery OEM orders and production schedules (Volvo's net orders plus 26 percent in Q3 2026; 2027 outlook 320,000 Europe, 300,000 North America; Daimler's North American deliveries plus 51 percent; Volvo CE orders plus 16 percent; AGCO underproducing retail by 15 percent) to harness, sheet-metal and system call-offs with a lag of one to two quarters to base-business revenue and plant utilisation (Mexico and Plattsburgh being the under-loaded sites). Direction clear; magnitude clouded by dealer inventories (3.3 months) and by AQ's share gains (e-truck harness nomination) that can offset a production dip.
  • Defence procurement (budgets up; German 2027 authorisations down 26 percent; Italy scaled back; Kitron's defence sales doubling) to AQ's defence deliveries with a long and lumpy lag (2025's bookings-to-invoicing gap; Q3 2026 deliveries slipping on customer equipment). Direction clear, timing poor; the SEK 60m capacity and the "nominations expected late 2026" are the markers.
  • Copper, steel and electronic-component prices (copper near records, steel up about 32 percent year to date, EU out-of-quota steel duty 50 percent from July 2026, onsemi raising prices 10 to 20 percent) to AQ's cost of semi-finished inputs to gross margin with a three-to-six-month pass-through lag; magnitude small (metal content about 2.4 percent of sales; 0.2 to 0.4 points of margin for one to two quarters) but the timing is now unfavourable because prices are rising into 2027.
  • The krona to reported revenue and EBIT by translation (73 percent of sales non-SEK, unhedged; 10 percent is about SEK 664m of revenue and roughly SEK 65m of EBIT, calculated at group margin), no lag; the model carries minus 0.5 percent for 2027.
  • Pillar II to tax rate: Swedish income-inclusion rule collecting top-up on Bulgarian (10 percent local rate, moving to 15 for large groups), Estonian (0 percent on retained profit) and Lithuanian (16 to 17 percent, so little top-up) profit after substance carve-outs that fall each year; accrued since 2025, first paid in 2027; the chain is clear, the quantum is the only unknown (DNB Carnegie holds 20 percent flat; the model 20.8 to 21.8 percent).
  • North American competitor capacity and the 800V/solid-state roadmap (Hammond Fort Worth from Q4 2027; Tamura Tijuana to four times FY2023 by FY2027; ABB's solid-state units from the second half of 2027; native 800V DC at 15 to 40 percent of 2030 new builds) to AQ's data-centre pricing and growth with a lag that starts late 2027 and runs to 2030; direction clear (negative for growth and price unless AQ is designed in), timing uncertain, outside the 18-month window.

Where the external chain is opaque. Who captures price in medium-voltage data-centre magnetics (ABB's contract terms with AQ are proprietary; Hammond and Tamura report price increases, AQ does not); the hyperscalers' real build schedules against grid and permitting constraints (a 57 GW shortfall through 2028; moratorium resolutions); and ABB's long-run sourcing intent. None of these can be read from a published number; all three are expert-call questions.

Net. A Consumer can write down, line by line, how a given hyperscaler capex figure, truck production figure, copper price and krona rate flow into AQ's EPS within a quarter or two, and can check the company's own claims against two published KPIs and a growth split every quarter. What the Consumer cannot do is verify the margin of the engine, which is the claim that carries the multiple. That asymmetry is the right summary of AQ's traceability: clear on volume and on the arithmetic below EBIT, inferred on mix.

D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

C58

Judgement: mostly idiosyncratically. Over the latest six months the dashboard peer basket explains 22 percent of AQ's daily variance (correlation 0.46, beta 0.91) and 78 percent is AQ's own; over two years the explained share has ranged from 5 to 34 percent and has never been above a third. What co-movement exists is thematic rather than industrial: the two peers AQ moves most with are the data-centre names (Huber+Suhner 0.44, Volex 0.42), not the industrial-cycle names (Bekaert 0.25, SFS 0.34, Comer 0.26). The multiple is therefore company-driven in the main, with a growing AI-infrastructure component that is the channel through which sector sentiment now reaches it; that is favourable for predictability while AQ delivers and is the mechanism of the October 2026 de-rating.

The co-movement evidence. Rolling six-month windows against an equal-weighted basket of thirteen peers (no price history for SKF or Trelleborg): correlation 0.46, beta 0.91, R-squared 22 percent at 23 October 2026; AQ plus 3.4 percent over the window against the basket's plus 4.3 percent. The 24 month-end readings show three phases: low co-movement through late 2024 and early 2025 (R-squared 3 to 8 percent, when AQ was de-rating on its own 2024 downturn and the share sat near SEK 132); a rise to 25 to 34 percent from April 2025 to September 2025 (the tariff shock and its recovery moved everything together); a fall to 13 percent in October 2025 (AQ's own Q3 2025 report and DNB Carnegie's upgrade to Buy); and a renewed rise to 31 to 33 percent in August and September 2026 as the AI-infrastructure complex traded as a block, easing to 22 percent in October when AQ fell about 9 percent from SEK 248 to SEK 227 while its estimates rose. Pairwise, the correlations that matter: Huber+Suhner 0.44 (R-squared 19 percent) and Volex 0.42 (18 percent), the sector's other designed-in data-centre suppliers; SFS 0.34 (12 percent); everything else below 0.3; Coats, TT Electronics, Daetwyler and Solid State essentially uncorrelated (0.04 to 0.10). Senior's beta of 2.38 is noise from its own volatility. In plain terms, when AQ moves with anyone it moves with the AI-power trade, and the AI-power trade is what the market has been selling in October 2026 on financing concerns.

What drives the multiple, read from the valuation series. The 24-month-forward P/E is 20.83 times, the 49th percentile of its own five-year range (15.54 to 25.66 times, median 20.91) and almost exactly its median; the dashboard's trailing-type P/E of 22.4 times sits at the 77th percentile of ten years, and the model uses a two-year-forward 21.5 times against a guideline fair multiple of 22.8 times. Three weeks ago the forward multiple was at the 91st percentile; the move from the top decile to the median happened with FY2027 consensus EPS up 7 to 8 percent in the month and the target price up 8 percent, all four brokers raising numbers on 15 and 16 October. That is a multiple falling for reasons outside the company's results, and the co-movement data say which reason: the thematic channel (Volex and Huber+Suhner), not the industrial channel. Over the longer two years, though, the multiple's main moves were company-driven: the fall to about 15.5 times in early 2025 followed AQ's own organic decline and Q4 2024 miss (minus 9 percent organic), and the rise to the mid-20s through 2026 followed AQ's own acceleration and the margin-target raise. So the record is: the level of the multiple is set by AQ's delivery, and the short-term swings around it increasingly by the AI-infrastructure trade.

Peers for context. Volex trades at 14.5 times at the 92nd percentile of its own history; Huber+Suhner at 17.0 times at the 11th; Trelleborg at 17.8 times at the 84th; SFS at 16.5 times at the 59th; the Nordic contract manufacturers on DNB Carnegie's table at 11.6 (Incap) to 20.8 times (Kitron) 2027 P/E against AQ's 22.8 times on the same table. AQ is the most expensive name in its Nordic cohort and the only one in its dashboard sector rated A for both technical and sell-side momentum with a D for valuation; the sector's own valuation dispersion is wide (P/E percentiles from 6 to 96), which is another way of saying these shares are priced on their own stories rather than on a sector multiple.

Implications for predictability. (1) Because the multiple is company-driven, it will respond to the things the forecasts say will happen: a clean Q4 beat (positive), then a visible deceleration in organic growth from mid-2027 (negative, even though it is in every forecast). The first set's "relabelling" point applies: AQ has already been re-rated from a contract manufacturer to an AI-power and defence beneficiary, and a company-driven multiple can be relabelled back if the growth rate, not the level, disappoints. (2) The thematic channel is one-directional in the current regime: sector digests have the market Underweight momentum and Double Underweight fragility, and AQ is a premium-multiple, momentum-A, AI-adjacent mid-cap, so a hyperscaler guidance cut or an 800V announcement can take the multiple down regardless of AQ's print, while a strong AI tape lifts it only modestly because the correlation is 0.4, not 0.8. (3) The downside cone of the multiple is wider than the earnings cone: the first set's trough arithmetic (15.5 to 16 times on intact earnings, about SEK 170; on cut earnings, about SEK 140) stands, against a fair range the first set put at 18 to 21 times and the model's guideline of 22.8 times. (4) For the Consumer's return work (topic #12): the model's 18-month total shareholder return of plus 21.7 percent at 22.8 times is almost all earnings (plus 21.3 points) with the multiple a small negative (minus 1.3); at the first set's 20 times the 18-month return is about plus 8 percent and at the five-year median of 20.9 times about plus 12 percent (calculated); at the trough it is negative. The multiple, not the earnings, decides whether the 18-month return is good or poor, and the multiple is set about three-quarters by AQ's own delivery and a quarter by a theme it does not control.

Layer 4: Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

The Investment Analyst's heading The fourth set: assessing the extent to which our assessment is reflected in guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative

The fourth set as a whole ❌ D47 written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Guidance, consensus and the narrative reflect our assessment of the next 18 months closely and our assessment of the following two years hardly at all; the gap is small and slightly favourable on the next two reports, and modestly unfavourable thereafter. The company gives no numbers, so there is nothing to beat except the sell-side's own conservative extrapolations, and those will probably be beaten in February and April 2027 and trimmed from July 2027 as the base business meets its comparisons, the tax note resets the FY2027 rate and the growth rate falls through the company's own 10 percent organic marker. Consensus EPS for FY2027-28 is 3 to 4 percent above our model for reasons that are dated and knowable, and the breadth of revisions, now four of four up, is likely to turn mixed by mid-2027 and net negative by the autumn. The narrative is one broker deep, positive, and confined to the next year; it does not contain the three dynamics that will decide 2028-30 (competitor capacity, the re-award, who holds the pricing rent), and it does not acknowledge that the 2031 EPS ambition is a balance-sheet decision rather than an operating forecast, though the sell-side has done that arithmetic.

What is favourable: the next two prints are more likely to beat than miss; the company has said it wants more acquisitions and consensus excludes them by convention, so any deal is an upgrade; the de-rating since 6 October has been thematic, not fundamental, and leaves the forward multiple at its five-year median; and the margin target is a floor the company already stands on. What is unfavourable: our operating numbers sit below the Street on the lines tested first; the deceleration that both we and consensus forecast is not in the narrative and will read as a slowdown; and the medium-term risks we weigh are unknown to the stock's holders, so they cannot be in the price. For Richard's purposes the gap is best described as follows: the market expects what we expect for the next six months, expects a little more than we do for the twelve months after that, and has not yet asked the questions that matter after 2028.

Guidance

1 question
I-0152
I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

C52

Judgement: AQ has no numeric guidance to raise, meet or reduce, so the question resolves into three others. Its long-term targets will be reaffirmed at every one of the six reports in the window (probability about 90 percent), the margin target will be met in most quarters but probably not for the FY2026 or FY2027 full year (model EBT 9.8 and 9.96 percent, a coin toss for FY2027), the growth target will be missed in both years unless acquisitions step up, and the only live candidates for a change are a further margin or capital-allocation statement around the FY2027 report in February 2028 (about 25 percent) and a quiet reframing of the 2031 EPS-doubling ambition if deals do not arrive (about 30 percent by 2028). Informal commentary, which is what moves this stock, is likely to stay above consensus on data centres and capacity through April 2027 and to turn cautious on the base business from the summer of 2027.

What exists to be raised, met or reduced. Targets, not guidance: EBT margin above 10 percent through the cycle (raised from 8 percent on 14 July 2026 after 13 quarters above the old level); sales growth above 15 percent a year (10 organic plus 5 acquired), missed in 2024 and 2025 and on course to be missed in 2026 (about 12.8 percent) and 2027 (model 10.0, consensus 11.2 percent); equity ratio above 40 percent (67 to 68 percent, met); dividend about 25 percent of cycle-average profit; doubling EPS every five years (achieved 2021 to the twelve months to Q3 2026; next by 2031); operational targets of 98 percent delivery precision, 100 percent quality and 3.5 inventory turns (none met). The company has changed a financial target once in several years and only after three years of evidence, so the prior for any formal change inside 18 months is low.

Event by event.

  • FY2026 report, 11 February 2027. Most likely outcome: a strong quarter (revenue about SEK 2,730m, plus 17.5 percent; EBIT about SEK 270m, 9.9 percent) that beats the consensus implied for Q4 (revenue about SEK 2,726m, EBIT about SEK 267m) by a little on revenue and more on EBIT if catch-up deliveries land cleanly; FY2026 EBT margin of about 9.8 percent, below the target for the year but with three of four quarters at or near 10 percent; dividend raised to about SEK 2.10; targets reaffirmed. Probability of a beat against consensus on EBIT: 55 to 60 percent, the record (6 and 16.7 percent beats in the last two quarters) tempered by Q4 being AQ's clean-up quarter (quality provisions in Q4 2025; SEK 185m of inventory write-downs in 2025; Time 24 losses; Norlake start-up). Drivers of a miss: a provision, a further defence slip, Mexico and Plattsburgh underutilisation. Possible additions: a first statement from the new chair on capital structure or a leverage framework (about 20 percent), and acquisition news (management's stated wish for more deals; about 30 percent of a deal announced with or around the report).
  • Q1 2027 report, 22 April 2027 (with the AGM). Reported growth about plus 16 percent against a Q1 2026 depressed by minus 4.8 percent of currency; organic about 12 to 13 percent; EBITDA margin about 13.4 percent; EBT about 9.9 percent. Probability of beating consensus: about 55 percent, slightly lower than February because the base's comparison begins to harden and because IFRS 18 restatements create presentation noise. Informal tone: capacity additions under way, Norlake's run-rate, data-centre demand still ahead of capacity. The first point at which the organic growth rate falls visibly from 17.9 percent, which the market may read as a slowdown even though it is in every model.
  • Q2 2027 report, mid-July 2027. Reported growth about plus 10 percent, organic about 6 to 7 percent, below the company's 10 percent organic target for the first time in six quarters; EBT about 10.2 percent on seasonal strength. Probability of a beat: about 45 percent. The quarter in which the base business's second-half direction becomes visible (truck production versus dealer destocking) and in which the FY2026 annual report's tax note has been digested. The margin target met for the quarter is likely (65 percent); the growth target plainly missed.
  • Q3 2027 report, mid-October 2027. The hardest comparison (Q3 2026 was flat on Q2 against a normal seasonal fall of 10 to 13 percent, on US and China utilisation management called partly temporary); model plus 6.8 percent reported, EBITDA margin 13.5 percent. Probability of a beat: about 40 percent, the lowest of the six; the quarter most likely to produce the first negative estimate revisions. Competitor capacity (Hammond's Fort Worth from Q4 2027) will be in the news by then.
  • FY2027 report, February 2028. The first test of a full year at or above 10 percent EBT (model 9.96 percent: a coin toss); if met, the probability of a further target step (for example "above 11 percent" or a capital markets day presenting the 2031 path) is about 25 percent, because the board raised the margin target only after a long record and the new chair has said the focus is profitable growth under challenging targets; if not met, the target is kept (it is "through the cycle") and the growth target is retained as aspiration. Any formal statement on leverage, buybacks or special dividends is more likely here than at any earlier event (about 25 percent); management's current position is no buybacks ("structurally low liquidity", weaker since Large Cap entry) and no stated net-debt target.
  • Q1 2028 report, April 2028. Model plus 7.5 percent; by then the market's question will be the 2028 data-centre growth rate against arriving competitor capacity and the re-award window.

What would raise, meet or reduce each target, in order of likelihood. Margin target met for a full year (FY2027): mix continuing (data centres plus 50 percent), Time 24 at break-even by mid-2027, delivery precision recovering, no Q4 provisions, acquired dilution of no more than 0.1 point; against it: a European volume downturn with 15 to 20 percent decremental margins, a copper or steel spike into 2027, Norlake start-up costs. Growth target met: only with acquired growth of 5 percent plus organic of 10, which needs both a step-up in deals (SEK 500m-plus of acquired revenue a year against SEK 100 to 300m in 2026) and a base business still growing mid-single digits; probability under 15 percent in either year. EPS-doubling ambition: on the model's path it is not reached (about SEK 13.5 against SEK 16.4 by 2031); the ambition survives if management announces larger acquisitions by 2028, and is reframed or allowed to lapse otherwise; management has not yet acknowledged publicly that the ambition depends on balance-sheet use, though the sell-side (DNB Carnegie's 8 percent acquired-growth scenario) has made the arithmetic explicit. Operational targets: delivery precision is likely to recover toward 94 to 95 percent over two to four quarters (the 2022-25 precedent) and the 98 percent target to remain unmet; inventory turns are likely to drift toward 3.1 to 3.2 as growth slows, not 3.5.

Informal commentary against consensus. Above consensus and likely to stay so through April 2027: data-centre demand "developing faster than expected", all data-centre factories at full throttle, Norlake "effectively doubling" US capacity, further capacity in 2027, backlog recovery in Q4, defence nominations, share gains, the wish for more deals. In line to cautious, and likely to become the dominant tone from July 2027: truck orders, Mexico and Plattsburgh, Time 24, semiconductor customers, the sustainability of Q3's utilisation, delivery precision. Because AQ gives no numbers, this tone is what the sell-side models and the market trade on; the pattern of the next 18 months is therefore two reports in which the company sounds better than consensus and three or four in which it sounds more cautious than its own run-rate, which is the inverse of the sell-side's current extrapolation.

The gap between our assessment and guidance, and how favourable it is. Our margin view (about 10 percent, held) matches the target read as a floor, so there is no cushion and no gap. Our growth view (10 percent in 2027, 8 percent in 2028) is below the company's target, but the market has long discounted the target, so the gap does not create a disappointment. Our capital-allocation view (no step-up in the base case) is below the company's ambition, which is the one place where the company says more than we forecast; if it acts, it is upside to our numbers and to consensus; if it does not, the ambition becomes a credibility cost in 2028-29, not in the window. The net is a neutral gap with a modest favourable tilt from the fact that the company's stated ambitions exceed our forecasts and are, at least on acquisitions, now backed by a stated intent to act.

Sell-side estimates

1 question
I-0246
I-02

How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

❌ D46

Judgement: consensus will most likely rise a little more through the February and April 2027 reports and then be trimmed through the second half of 2027, so that by the spring of 2028 FY2027 EPS stands within a few percent of today's SEK 10.15 (my probabilities: 30 percent higher by more than 2 percent, 30 percent within 2 percent, 40 percent lower by more than 2 percent) and FY2028 EPS a little below today's SEK 11.05. Net breadth follows the same shape: all four brokers up at the next two events (as they were on 15 and 16 October), then mixed, then net down in the autumn of 2027. The three mechanisms that will cut consensus are already in our model (tax, the base business's comparisons, a smaller margin step); the one mechanism that would raise it is outside both consensus and our base case (acquisitions, which consensus models exclude by convention). Our assessment is therefore 3 to 4 percent below consensus for FY2027-28 on operations and above it on optionality.

Where consensus stands and how it is built. FactSet median: revenue SEK 10,226m, 11,367m, 12,216m, 13,291m; EBIT SEK 1,006m, 1,150m, 1,266m, 1,370m; EPS SEK 8.77, 10.15, 11.05, 11.80 (FY2026-29; four brokers for the first three years, one for FY2029). Implied EBIT margins 9.8, 10.1, 10.4 percent; implied tax 20.3, 19.7, 20.2 percent (calculated; consensus's own medians do not fully reconcile, FY2027 net income over EPS implying 90.6m shares against 91.7m in issue). The four visible brokers are within SEK 0.40 of each other on FY2027 EPS (DNB Carnegie 10.00, Pareto 10.11, the restricted house 10.20, Nordea 10.40) and all rate the shares Hold with targets of SEK 232 to 260 (mean 257, 13 percent above the price). DNB Carnegie's published model (the only one visible in detail) has FY2027 revenue of SEK 11,254m, identical to ours, an EBITA margin of 10.1 percent, a tax rate flat at 20 percent, cash conversion of 50 percent long term and no unannounced acquisitions in the base, with a separate scenario of 8 percent acquired growth a year that yields a fair value of SEK 242. Revisions over the last twelve months: FY2027 EPS plus 7, 9, 12 and 11 percent over one, three, six and twelve months; sales plus 6, 7, 11, 10; EBITDA plus 6, 7, 11, 9; target price plus 8, 9, 13; buy share unchanged at 33 percent; the dashboard's revision-momentum score of 12 is level with Volex and second only to MTU in the sector. The year's shape was a trim (FY2026 from about SEK 8.38 to a low of 7.94 by the spring, on tax and a soft Q4 2025) followed by two beats that lifted every year; the setup is upgrade momentum, not cleared expectations.

The direction, event by event.

  • 11 February 2027 (FY2026). The quarter is likely to beat on revenue (catch-up deliveries; Norlake's first quarter) and on EBIT by a few percent, and FY2026 EPS lands near SEK 8.8 to 8.9 against 8.77. Mechanical effect: FY2027 revenue and EPS nudged up 1 to 3 percent as brokers roll the higher exit rate, and FY2028 initiated more carefully. Two things could cap it: a Q4 provision, and the FY2026 tax rate printing at 20 percent or above, which forces the FY2027 line up from the implied 19.7 percent. Breadth: probably four of four up. Probability consensus FY2027 EPS is higher a month after this report than today: 60 percent.
  • 22 April 2027 (Q1 2027). Growth about plus 16 percent reported; margin about 9.9 percent; most likely in line to slightly ahead. Brokers hold or trim FY2027 slightly as the organic rate decelerates to 12 to 13 percent and as the annual report's tax note (week 13) is incorporated; DNB Carnegie's flat 20 percent is the line most exposed. Breadth: mixed, net slightly positive. Probability FY2027 EPS higher than today after this report: 50 percent.
  • Mid-July 2027 (Q2 2027). Organic growth below 10 percent for the first time; the base business's second-half direction visible; the cash-tax step in the half-year cash flow. Most likely a small trim to FY2027 (1 to 3 percent) and to FY2028 (2 to 4 percent), on tax and on the revenue exit rate; a margin at or above 10 percent limits the damage. Breadth: net negative. Probability FY2027 EPS higher than today after this report: 40 percent.
  • Mid-October 2027 (Q3 2027). The hardest comparison and the point at which Hammond's and Tamura's capacity and ABB's solid-state launch are in the newsflow; the probability of a miss is highest. Most likely a further 1 to 3 percent trim and FY2028 margin assumptions (10.4 percent) revisited toward 10.2. Breadth: net negative. Probability FY2027 EPS higher than today: 35 percent.
  • February and April 2028 (FY2027 and Q1 2028). FY2027 settles; attention moves to FY2028-29, where the sell-side's data-centre growth and margin assumptions meet competitor capacity and the re-award debate. Consensus FY2028 EPS most likely ends the window at SEK 10.6 to 10.9 against 11.05 today (ours 10.77), unless acquisitions have been announced.

Why consensus is more likely to be trimmed than raised on operations. (1) Tax: consensus implies the rate falls in FY2027 to 19.7 percent despite a nine-month 2026 rate of 20 percent that already includes Pillar II accruals and carve-outs that decline each year; a reset to 20.5 to 21 percent is worth 1.5 to 2 percent of FY2027-28 EPS, and the FY2026 tax note is the dated trigger. (2) Revenue: consensus's SEK 11,367m for FY2027 (plus 11.2 percent) needs about 10 percent organic growth with no unannounced acquisitions; the model's plus 7.5 percent organic is the sum of a capacity-bound slice (plus 50 percent on data centres) and a base at plus 3 percent; for consensus to be right the base must grow 6 to 7 percent through the year, which the first half may deliver and the second half probably will not. (3) Margin: consensus's 10.1 percent for FY2027 and 10.4 for FY2028 against the model's 10.0 and 10.2; the 2027 gap is small, the 2028 gap depends on mix continuing and on no price give-back as competitor capacity arrives. (4) Cash: consensus free cash flow (SEK 406m, 648m, 866m) does not reconcile with its own operating cash flow less capex, so it is a weak anchor; the 2027 cash-tax step is probably not in it.

Why consensus could instead be raised. (1) Acquisitions: excluded by convention; management now says it wants more; every deal is an automatic upgrade to revenue and, after dilution, to EPS; SEK 500m of acquired revenue at a 5 percent margin converging to 9 percent adds 1 to 3 percent to EPS over two years and 4 percent to revenue. The probability of at least SEK 300m of acquired revenue announced inside 18 months I put at 55 percent, of SEK 1bn or more at 20 percent. (2) Data-centre capacity landing faster: Norlake at a USD 20m-plus run-rate by mid-2027 and the 2027 transformer lines on time would push the slice above the model's SEK 1,915m for 2027 and support the consensus revenue line. (3) The base: Volvo's order intake up 26 percent, North American Class 8 production up 13 percent in 2027, construction orders up 16 percent and the US short-cycle index 62 percent above 2019 could keep the base at mid-single-digit growth through 2027 if dealer destocking is mild. (4) Pricing: repricing of data-centre contracts at 2027 renewals now that sales are spread across several customers (3 to 5 percent on about SEK 1bn is SEK 30 to 50m of EBIT, 3 to 4 percent of EPS); not in any model, including ours.

Breadth and ratings. Four brokers, all Hold, with a mean rating of Overweight (1.67) on FactSet's wider count and a buy share of 33 percent, which implies two or more brokers beyond the visible four. Ratings here have moved with price, not fundamentals (DNB Carnegie: Buy in October 2025 at a lower price, Hold from April 2026 as the shares rose); so a further fall in the share price toward SEK 200 would likely produce an upgrade or two on valuation, and a rally above SEK 260 a downgrade, independent of estimates. The net breadth of estimate revisions, by contrast, follows the prints: positive at the next two, negative at the following two. The dashboard's sell-side momentum rating of A will most likely be a B or C by the autumn of 2027.

18 months to three years out. FY2028 and FY2029 consensus (EPS SEK 11.05 and 11.80, the latter one broker) will be initiated by the other houses during 2027 and will inherit the sell-side's current extrapolation of data-centre growth and a 10.4 percent margin. Our view is 2.5 and 1.1 percent below. The direction of those years is a tug of war between a probable trim on margin and tax (minus 3 to 5 percent) and a possible lift from acquisitions (plus 2 to 6 percent if the step-up happens); the balance is "held, with a wider dispersion" rather than a clear cut or raise. The sell-side's FY2029 figure (plus 6.8 percent EPS growth) already encodes a maturing story.

The gap and its favourability. On operations our assessment sits 3 to 4 percent below consensus EPS for FY2027-28 for reasons that will surface on a known calendar (the tax note in March, the comparisons from July), which is unfavourable: it means the probable path of estimates over the window is down, and this stock's multiple is company-driven and sensitive to the direction of revisions. Against that, the next two reports are more likely to beat than miss, acquisitions are a live and now stated upside that consensus omits, and consensus already carries deceleration (growth 7.5 percent in FY2028) and no expectations cushion was ever built in by guidance. The net is a modestly unfavourable gap: our numbers are below the Street on the lines that will be tested first.

Stock market narrative

1 question
I-0345
I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

❌ D45

Judgement: the narrative is thin, uniform and front-loaded. One house (DNB Carnegie) supplies almost all of the articulated commentary; the three other covering houses are restricted or unseen; the AlphaSense search for investor commentary (X, Nordic forums, newsletters) found nothing attributable, so the investor narrative for this Large Cap entrant is effectively undocumented. Within the sell-side narrative, every positive dynamic for the next 18 months is well known and priced into estimates (data-centre ramp, Q4 catch-up, capacity doubling, the margin target, M&A optionality), the near-term negatives are partly known (working capital, delivery precision, tax as a level but not as a trajectory, acquired dilution), and the 18-to-36-month dynamics that our assessment weighs most heavily are discussed by one house or by none (who captures data-centre pricing, North American competitor capacity from late 2027, the 800V and solid-state re-award, the composition of 2026 growth, the Halma precedent for valuing single-customer data-centre revenue). The market knows the good news and not the medium-term bad news, which makes the gap unfavourable for the case, offset only by the October de-rating having run ahead of anything in the fundamentals.

Who speaks. Covering houses identified: DNB Carnegie (detailed, frequent, the source of nearly every quantified view in the narrative report), Nordea Equity Research (estimates visible, research restricted), Pareto Securities (same), one house FactSet does not name; ABG Sundal Collier, Inderes and Redeye publish on peers (Inission, Scanfil, Incap, HANZA, Lifco) but no AQ document was found. Investor commentary: none found on X, Nordic forums or newsletters by the search commissioned for this work; the only "investor" signals in the files are the ownership facts (founders 17.2 and 17.1 percent; no insider sales; the dashboard's interest level B and Stage 2 bucket "proven") and the price action (minus 9 percent in three weeks while estimates rose). This matters for the memo's topic #8: narrative momentum cannot be measured from investor commentary here, only inferred from price, revisions and one broker's tone.

Dynamic by dynamic: who discusses it, how prominently, with what view, and whether our assessment differs.

Dynamic (our assessment in brackets) Sell-side Investors How well known
Data-centre ramp as the growth engine; demand ahead of capacity; Norlake doubling US capacity (agree; capacity-bound for 18 months) DNB Carnegie, prominently, every note since 2025; Nordea and Pareto estimates consistent Implicit in the premium multiple Fully known and in estimates
Margin target above 10 percent as sustainable (agree as a floor; a full year not yet printed) DNB Carnegie: sustainable, 10.1 percent in 2027; had argued the opposite in May 2025 Priced Known; the full-year test is not discussed
Composition of Q3 2026 growth: base about 12 percent organic, data centres about 3 points (we see the base, not data centres, as the deceleration risk) No house decomposes it; DNB Carnegie attributes growth to data centres and defence; management cited share gains and US/China utilisation None Not discussed; the sell-side's framing understates base-business risk and overstates data-centre dependence
Deceleration of organic growth below 10 percent from mid-2027 (agree; scheduled) Implicit in consensus growth of 7.5 percent for FY2028; not framed as an event None Known in the numbers, not in the narrative; a prime candidate for a "slowdown" headline in July 2027
Who captures medium-voltage data-centre pricing: ABB or AQ (we think ABB; AQ takes volume) No house; DNB Carnegie discusses pass-through lags only; expert calls say OEMs capture the premium None Not discussed
North American competitor capacity from late 2027 (Hammond, Tamura, Forgent, Eaton, Schneider, Siemens) (we carry a halving of data-centre growth in 2028) None of the AQ coverage mentions it; it is in US and Japanese research on the competitors None Unknown to AQ's investors
800V DC and solid-state re-award; AQ's prototypes (binary for 2028-30; not in the 18-month window) DNB Carnegie frames 800V as an opportunity from 2028; the risk side "mostly quiet"; ABB's H2 2027 solid-state launch covered by Deutsche Bank and Bernstein on ABB, not on AQ None Partly known as upside, not as risk
Pillar II: level versus trajectory (we have 20.8 to 21.8 percent; consensus flat at about 20) DNB Carnegie models 20 percent flat and flags cash tax rising to SEK 218m in 2027; the trajectory of carve-outs not discussed None Known as a level; the trajectory and the 2027 cash step are not
Working-capital absorption and cash conversion (agree it is the weak link; counter-cyclical release) DNB Carnegie: 50 percent long-run cash conversion; Q2 cash drain discussed; peers' hub and deposit models not applied to AQ None Known
Gävle gain (in 2025, in financial items) DNB Carnegie adjusted correctly at the time None Known to the sell-side; our own earlier sets had it wrong
Delivery precision 91.1 percent and the quality precedent (SEK 250m account lost in 2023) DNB Carnegie: "unacceptable", risk to reputation, recovery unlikely to be swift None Known
Customer concentration in data centres; the Halma Avo precedent (one customer, 13 times against 19.4 times) DNB Carnegie downplays concentration (large diversified customers); nobody applies the Halma discount to AQ None Partly known; the valuation precedent is not applied
Unused net cash; 2031 EPS doubling needs about 8 percent a year of acquired growth (agree; not in our base case) DNB Carnegie, explicitly and prominently, with an 8 percent M&A scenario and a note that "a more meaningful step-up" is required; management has not said so itself None Known to the sell-side; the dependence is not acknowledged by the company
Founder ownership and no buybacks (agree; conservative stewardship) DNB Carnegie notes alignment; raises the possibility of buybacks or special dividends as the equity ratio rises None Known
Base-business cycle: trucks, construction, agriculture (our main 18-month variable) DNB Carnegie names commercial vehicles and European construction sentiment as "the key risk" to consensus None Known as a risk label, not quantified
The AI-financing risk-off and AQ's thematic co-movement (we see it as the main share-price risk) Sector digests, not AQ coverage The price action itself Known at sector level; not connected to AQ by any covering house

Reading the pattern. Everything the sell-side says about AQ is about the next two to four quarters and is positive or neutral: backlog, capacity, margin, cash, M&A optionality. The things that could change the 2028-30 trajectory (capacity arrival, re-award, pricing capture, ABB's sourcing intent) are absent from AQ's coverage and present only in research on other companies, so an investor who reads AQ's own coverage sees a compounder with an AI tailwind and no expiry date. Our assessment agrees with the sell-side on almost all the near-term dynamics and differs on three medium-term ones (we fade data-centre growth to plus 20 percent in 2028 and plus 10 thereafter, ease the slice's margin, and carry tax rising) and on one near-term framing (the base, not data centres, drove Q3 and will drive the deceleration). Where the sell-side is more positive than we are, it is in the parts of the forecast that will be tested last.

Peers and the sector narrative, for calibration. The sector digest compares AQ with Volex and Huber+Suhner as designed-in data-centre suppliers that the market still values "as cable assemblers rather than on the Amphenol label"; AQ is the exception that has been relabelled (22.8 times 2027 P/E against Volex's 14.6 times and Incap's 11.6 times). Halma's Avo shows how the market treats single-customer data-centre revenue when it becomes large (a 25 percent discount to the group multiple), a frame nobody has yet applied to AQ because the slice is still 6 to 7 percent of sales. Kitron's narrative (defence, backlog, estimate momentum) and Scanfil's (15 percent growth target, acquisitions) are the Nordic comparators the same analysts cover, and both are discussed with more quantified forward indicators (backlog, book-to-bill) than AQ offers, which makes AQ's narrative more dependent on tone. The October 2026 regime (AI infrastructure sold on financing; rotation from premium compounders to cheaper cyclicals; the dashboard's momentum Underweight and fragility Double Underweight) has reached AQ through the thematic channel without any AQ-specific commentary, which means the de-rating so far is a sentiment event with no fundamental articulation to reverse it or deepen it.

For 18 months to three years out. The sell-side narrative extends to 2031 only through the EPS-doubling arithmetic and the M&A scenario; the competitive and technological dynamics that decide whether the data-centre line keeps compounding after 2028 are not part of AQ's narrative at all. That is the largest unarticulated risk in the case, and also the largest unarticulated opportunity if AQ is designed into the solid-state generation, because neither branch is priced.

The gap and its favourability. For the next 18 months, the dynamics in our assessment are well articulated where they are positive and partly articulated where they are negative; the one framing that is ours alone (base-driven growth and a scheduled deceleration) is a negative for the narrative when it surfaces. For 18 to 36 months, the dynamics that our assessment weighs are largely unknown to AQ's investors and are mostly negative for the trajectory the market extrapolates. The offset is that the share price has already fallen 9 percent on a sector theme with estimates rising, so near-term sentiment is more negative than near-term fundamentals. Net: an unfavourable gap on the medium term, a mildly favourable one on the next two quarters.
