Input FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)
Working capital (% of revenue) 29.5 29.3 28.8 28.5 28.5

The model's cash flow treats every point of this ratio against FactSet's FY2025 year-end three-line balance (29.3 percent) as cash absorbed, which double-counts working capital acquired with Time 24 and Norlake and ignores the other operating liabilities that grow with revenue, so the previous 31.0 percent produced a full-year FY2026 operating cash flow of SEK 649m against SEK 631m already reported for nine months; the line is lowered by 1.5 points in every year to calibrate the model's absorption to the company's cash-flow statement while keeping the second set's year-on-year profile (on FactSet's balance-sheet definition I still expect about 31 percent at end-2026 easing to about 30 percent by 2029).

Working capital is the balance at the end of each fiscal year as a percentage of the last twelve months' revenue.

Co-movement of AQ-SE with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-10-23 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: AQ Group AB (AQ-SE, Nasdaq Stockholm Large Cap): a decentralised Swedish contract manufacturer of components and systems for industrial OEMs, with more than 40 operating companies in 17 countries; Component segment (inductive components and transformers including medium-voltage units for data-centre UPS systems, wiring harnesses, sheet metal, injection moulding) and System segment (electrical cabinets and complete system assembly), serving commercial vehicles, construction and agricultural machinery, rail, defence, grid and data-centre power customers.

Request: OWN-1

Question: What do investors in AQ Group (AQ-SE) currently say and do: which institutional funds and founders hold the shares and how have their positions changed since the company's move to Nasdaq Stockholm Large Cap on 2 January 2026 and since its Q3 2026 report of 15 October 2026 (holdings data from Modular Finance or Holdings, fund reports and 13F-type filings); what Nordic investor commentary exists on X/Twitter, Avanza and Nordnet forums, Placera, Affärsvärlden, Dagens Industri, Börsveckan, EFN and fund managers' letters about the company's data-centre transformer business, its raised pre-tax margin target of above 10 percent (14 July 2026), its acquisitions and its share-price fall from SEK 248 on 6 October 2026 to about SEK 227 on 23 October 2026; which of these investors treat the company as an AI-infrastructure stock, a defence stock or an industrial compounder; and how investors in comparable Nordic names (Kitron, Scanfil, Note, HANZA, Lifco, Addtech) and in data-centre component suppliers (Volex, Huber+Suhner) have repositioned during the October 2026 sell-off in AI-infrastructure equities?

Request: OWN-2

Question: How do AQ Group's (AQ-SE) customers rate its delivery performance and quality today, given the company's reported delivery precision of 91.1 percent in Q3 2026 against a 98 percent target (interim report, 15 October 2026), its quality provisions in the Inductive Components division in Q4 2025 (year-end report, 12 February 2026) and its loss of a battery-system account in Bulgaria over quality in 2023 (Q4 2023 earnings call, 15 February 2024): what do procurement, supplier-quality and engineering staff at Volvo Group, Scania and Traton, Daimler Truck, Volvo Construction Equipment, Alstom, Hitachi Energy, ABB Electrification and Siemens say about AQ as a supplier of wiring harnesses, sheet metal, electrical cabinets and medium-voltage transformers and inductors; is any re-sourcing, dual-sourcing or programme re-award involving AQ under way or being considered; how long did comparable delivery-precision recoveries take at AQ in 2017 and 2022-2025 and at Kitron, Scanfil, Note and HANZA; and which OEM programmes (including the new electric-truck harness nomination disclosed in Q3 2026 and the medium-voltage data-centre transformer programmes) come up for renewal or re-award in 2027 and 2028?

Request: OWN-3

Question: How have the forward price-to-earnings and EV/EBITA multiples of AQ Group (AQ-SE) and of comparable companies behaved in the twelve to twenty-four months after a peak in organic growth, and what phase of the stock lifecycle did each case correspond to: for AQ Group itself after its 2022-2023 growth peak (organic growth of 20.7 percent in 2023 per the 2023 annual report, then negative organic growth in 2024), for Nordic contract manufacturers (Kitron, Scanfil, Note, HANZA, Incap) after their 2022-2023 peaks and through the 2024 destocking, for Nordic serial acquirers (Lifco, Addtech, Lagercrantz, Indutrade) through the 2022 rate shock and the 2024-2025 slowdown, for data-centre and electrification component suppliers (Volex, Huber+Suhner, Hammond Power Solutions, Tamura) in 2025-2026, and for European engineered-component makers (SFS Group, Bekaert, Daetwyler, Comer Industries) in 2023-2025; in each case, what multiple range did the shares trade at while delivery was strong, what multiple did they fall to when growth decelerated or estimates were cut, how long the de-rating lasted, and what distinguished the cases where the multiple recovered from those where it did not?
